```
    34→def _image_to_frame_simple(image_path: str | Path, target_size: tuple[int, int]) -> np.ndarray | None:
    35→    """Loads an image, resizes it, and converts to BGR NumPy array for OpenCV.
    36→
    37→    This is the original simple version kept as a private helper.  The public
    38→    ``image_to_frame`` function (below) supports URL downloading and is the
    39→    one exported by the package.
    40→    """
    41→    try:
    42→        img = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
    43→        img = img.resize(target_size, Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)
    44→        return cv2.cvtColor(np.array(img), cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR)
    45→    except FileNotFoundError:
    46→        headless_logger.error(f"Image file not found at {image_path}")
    47→        return None
    48→    except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
    49→        headless_logger.error(f"Error loading or processing image {image_path}: {e}")
    50→        return None
    51→
    52→def create_color_frame(size: tuple[int, int], color_bgr: tuple[int, int, int] = (0, 0, 0)) -> np.ndarray:
    53→    """Creates a single color BGR frame (default black)."""
    54→    height, width = size[1], size[0] # size is (width, height)
    55→    frame = np.full((height, width, 3), color_bgr, dtype=np.uint8)
    56→    return frame
    57→
    58→def get_easing_function(name: str):
    59→    """
    60→    Returns an easing function by name.
    61→    """
    62→    if name == 'linear':
    63→        return lambda t: t
    64→    elif name == 'ease_in_quad':
    65→        return lambda t: t * t
    66→    elif name == 'ease_out_quad':
    67→        return lambda t: t * (2 - t)
    68→    elif name == 'ease_in_out_quad' or name == 'ease_in_out': # Added alias
    69→        return lambda t: 2 * t * t if t < 0.5 else -1 + (4 - 2 * t) * t
    70→    elif name == 'ease_in_cubic':
    71→        return lambda t: t * t * t
    72→    elif name == 'ease_out_cubic':
    73→        return lambda t: 1 + ((t - 1) ** 3)
    74→    elif name == 'ease_in_out_cubic':
    75→        return lambda t: 4 * t * t * t if t < 0.5 else 1 + ((2 * t - 2) ** 3) / 2
    76→    elif name == 'ease_in_quart':
    77→        return lambda t: t * t * t * t
    78→    elif name == 'ease_out_quart':
    79→        return lambda t: 1 - ((t - 1) ** 4)
    80→    elif name == 'ease_in_out_quart':
    81→        return lambda t: 8 * t * t * t * t if t < 0.5 else 1 - ((-2 * t + 2) ** 4) / 2
    82→    elif name == 'ease_in_quint':
    83→        return lambda t: t * t * t * t * t
    84→    elif name == 'ease_out_quint':
    85→        return lambda t: 1 + ((t - 1) ** 5)
    86→    elif name == 'ease_in_out_quint':
    87→        return lambda t: 16 * t * t * t * t * t if t < 0.5 else 1 + ((-2 * t + 2) ** 5) / 2
    88→    elif name == 'ease_in_sine':
    89→        return lambda t: 1 - math.cos(t * math.pi / 2)
    90→    elif name == 'ease_out_sine':
    91→        return lambda t: math.sin(t * math.pi / 2)
    92→    elif name == 'ease_in_out_sine':
    93→        return lambda t: -(math.cos(math.pi * t) - 1) / 2
    94→    elif name == 'ease_in_expo':
    95→        return lambda t: 0 if t == 0 else 2 ** (10 * (t - 1))
    96→    elif name == 'ease_out_expo':
    97→        return lambda t: 1 if t == 1 else 1 - 2 ** (-10 * t)
    98→    elif name == 'ease_in_out_expo':
    99→        if t == 0: return 0
   100→        if t == 1: return 1
   101→        if t < 0.5:
   102→            return (2 ** (20 * t - 10)) / 2
   103→        else:
   104→            return (2 - 2 ** (-20 * t + 10)) / 2
   105→    else: # Default to ease_in_out
   106→        return lambda t: 2 * t * t if t < 0.5 else -1 + (4 - 2 * t) * t
   107→
   108→def create_video_from_frames_list(
   109→    frames_list: list[np.ndarray],
   110→    output_path: str | Path,
   111→    fps: int,
   112→    resolution: tuple[int, int] # width, height
   113→):
   114→    """Creates an MP4 video from a list of NumPy BGR frames.
   115→
   116→    This is a wrapper around source.media.video.create_video_from_frames_list
   117→    with BT.709 colorspace standardization enabled by default.
   118→    """
   119→    # Import here to avoid circular imports
   120→    from source.media.video import create_video_from_frames_list as _create_video
   121→    return _create_video(
   122→        frames_list,
   123→        output_path,
   124→        fps,
   125→        resolution,
   126→        standardize_colorspace=True
   127→    )
   128→
   129→def extract_video_segment_ffmpeg(
   130→    input_video_path: str | Path,
   131→    output_video_path: str | Path,
   132→    start_frame_index: int, # 0-indexed
   133→    num_frames_to_keep: int,
   134→    input_fps: float, # FPS of the input video for accurate -ss calculation
   135→    resolution: tuple[int,int]
   136→):
   137→    """Extracts a video segment using FFmpeg with stream copy if possible."""
   138→    headless_logger.debug(f"EXTRACT_VIDEO_SEGMENT_FFMPEG: Called with input='{input_video_path}', output='{output_video_path}', start_idx={start_frame_index}, num_frames={num_frames_to_keep}, input_fps={input_fps}")
   139→    if num_frames_to_keep <= 0:
   140→        headless_logger.warning(f"num_frames_to_keep is {num_frames_to_keep} for {output_video_path} (FFmpeg). Nothing to extract.")
   141→        headless_logger.debug("EXTRACT_VIDEO_SEGMENT_FFMPEG: num_frames_to_keep is 0 or less, returning.")
   142→        Path(output_video_path).unlink(missing_ok=True)
   143→        return
   144→
   145→    input_video_path = Path(input_video_path)
   146→    output_video_path = Path(output_video_path)
   147→    output_video_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   148→
   149→    start_time_seconds = start_frame_index / input_fps
   150→
   151→    cmd = [
   152→        'ffmpeg',
   153→        '-y',
   154→        '-ss', str(start_time_seconds),
   155→        '-i', str(input_video_path.resolve()),
   156→        '-vframes', str(num_frames_to_keep),
   157→        '-an',
   158→        str(output_video_path.resolve())
   159→    ]
   160→
   161→    headless_logger.debug(f"EXTRACT_VIDEO_SEGMENT_FFMPEG: Running command: {' '.join(cmd)}")
   162→    try:
   163→        process = subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, capture_output=True, text=True, encoding='utf-8')
   164→        headless_logger.debug(f"EXTRACT_VIDEO_SEGMENT_FFMPEG: Successfully extracted segment to {output_video_path}")
   165→        if process.stderr:
   166→            headless_logger.debug(f"FFmpeg stderr (for {output_video_path}):\n{process.stderr}")
   167→        if not output_video_path.exists() or output_video_path.stat().st_size == 0:
   168→            headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg command for {output_video_path} apparently succeeded but output file is missing or empty.")
   169→            headless_logger.debug(f"FFmpeg command for {output_video_path} produced no output. stdout:\n{process.stdout}\nstderr:\n{process.stderr}")
   170→
   171→    except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
   172→        headless_logger.error(f"Error during FFmpeg segment extraction for {output_video_path}:")
   173→        headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg command: {' '.join(e.cmd)}")
   174→        if e.stdout: headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg stdout:\n{e.stdout}")
   175→        if e.stderr: headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg stderr:\n{e.stderr}")
   176→        headless_logger.debug(f"FFmpeg extraction failed for {output_video_path}. Error: {e}")
   177→        Path(output_video_path).unlink(missing_ok=True)
   178→    except FileNotFoundError:
   179→        headless_logger.error("ffmpeg command not found. Please ensure ffmpeg is installed and in your PATH.")
   180→        headless_logger.debug("FFmpeg command not found during segment extraction.")
   181→        raise
   182→
   183→def stitch_videos_ffmpeg(video_paths_list: list[str | Path], output_path: str | Path):
   184→    output_path = Path(output_path)
   185→    output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   186→
   187→    if not video_paths_list:
   188→        headless_logger.warning("No videos to stitch.")
   189→        return
   190→
   191→    valid_video_paths = []
   192→    for p in video_paths_list:
   193→        resolved_p = Path(p).resolve()
   194→        if resolved_p.exists() and resolved_p.stat().st_size > 0:
   195→            valid_video_paths.append(resolved_p)
   196→        else:
   197→            headless_logger.warning(f"Video segment {resolved_p} is missing or empty. Skipping from stitch list.")
   198→
   199→    if not valid_video_paths:
   200→        headless_logger.warning("No valid video segments found to stitch after checks.")
   201→        return
   202→
   203→    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="ffmpeg_concat_") as tmpdir:
   204→        filelist_path = Path(tmpdir) / "ffmpeg_filelist.txt"
   205→        with open(filelist_path, 'w', encoding='utf-8') as f:
   206→            for video_path in valid_video_paths:
   207→                f.write(f"file '{video_path.as_posix()}'\n")
   208→
   209→        cmd = [
   210→            'ffmpeg', '-y', '-f', 'concat', '-safe', '0',
   211→            '-i', str(filelist_path),
   212→            '-c', 'copy', str(output_path)
   213→        ]
   214→
   215→        headless_logger.debug(f"Running ffmpeg to stitch videos: {' '.join(cmd)}")
   216→        try:
   217→            process = subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, capture_output=True, text=True, encoding='utf-8')
   218→            headless_logger.essential(f"Successfully stitched videos into: {output_path}")
   219→            if process.stderr: headless_logger.debug(f"FFmpeg log (stderr):\n{process.stderr}")
   220→        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
   221→            headless_logger.error(f"Error during ffmpeg stitching for {output_path}:")
   222→            headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg command: {' '.join(e.cmd)}")
   223→            if e.stdout: headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg stdout:\n{e.stdout}")
   224→            if e.stderr: headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg stderr:\n{e.stderr}")
   225→            raise
   226→        except FileNotFoundError:
   227→            headless_logger.error("ffmpeg command not found. Please ensure ffmpeg is installed and in your PATH.")
   228→            raise
   229→
   230→def save_frame_from_video(input_video_path: Path, frame_index: int, output_image_path: Path, resolution: tuple[int, int]):
   231→    """Extracts a specific frame from a video, resizes, and saves it as an image."""
   232→    headless_logger.debug(f"SAVE_FRAME_FROM_VIDEO: Input='{input_video_path}', Index={frame_index}, Output='{output_image_path}', Res={resolution}")
   233→    if not input_video_path.exists() or input_video_path.stat().st_size == 0:
   234→        headless_logger.error(f"Video file for frame extraction not found or empty: {input_video_path}")
   235→        return False
   236→
   237→    cap = cv2.VideoCapture(str(input_video_path))
   238→    if not cap.isOpened():
   239→        headless_logger.error(f"Could not open video file: {input_video_path}")
   240→        return False
   241→
   242→    total_frames = int(cap.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_COUNT))
   243→
   244→    # Allow Python-style negative indexing (e.g. -1 for last frame)
   245→    if frame_index < 0:
   246→        frame_index = total_frames + frame_index  # Convert to positive index
   247→
   248→    if frame_index < 0 or frame_index >= total_frames:
   249→        headless_logger.error(f"Frame index {frame_index} is out of bounds for video {input_video_path} (total frames: {total_frames}).")
   250→        cap.release()
   251→        return False
   252→
   253→    cap.set(cv2.CAP_PROP_POS_FRAMES, float(frame_index))
   254→    ret, frame = cap.read()
   255→    cap.release()
   256→
   257→    if not ret or frame is None:
   258→        headless_logger.error(f"Could not read frame {frame_index} from {input_video_path}.")
   259→        return False
   260→
   261→    try:
   262→        if frame.shape[1] != resolution[0] or frame.shape[0] != resolution[1]:
   263→            headless_logger.debug(f"SAVE_FRAME_FROM_VIDEO: Resizing frame from {frame.shape[:2]} to {resolution[:2][::-1]}")
   264→            frame = cv2.resize(frame, resolution, interpolation=cv2.INTER_AREA)
   265→
   266→        output_image_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   267→        cv2.imwrite(str(output_image_path), frame)
   268→        headless_logger.essential(f"Successfully saved frame {frame_index} from {input_video_path} to {output_image_path}")
   269→        return True
   270→    except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
   271→        headless_logger.error(f"Error saving frame to {output_image_path}: {e}")
   272→        traceback.print_exc()
   273→        return False
   274→
   275→# --- FFMPEG-based specific frame extraction ---
   276→def extract_specific_frame_ffmpeg(
   277→    input_video_path: str | Path,
   278→    frame_number: int, # 0-indexed
   279→    output_image_path: str | Path,
   280→    input_fps: float # Passed by caller, though not strictly needed for ffmpeg frame index selection using 'eq(n,frame_number)'
   281→):
   282→    """Extracts a specific frame from a video using FFmpeg and saves it as an image."""
   283→    headless_logger.debug(f"EXTRACT_SPECIFIC_FRAME_FFMPEG: Input='{input_video_path}', Frame={frame_number}, Output='{output_image_path}'")
   284→    input_video_p = Path(input_video_path)
   285→    output_image_p = Path(output_image_path)
   286→    output_image_p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   287→
   288→    if not input_video_p.exists() or input_video_p.stat().st_size == 0:
   289→        headless_logger.error(f"Input video for frame extraction not found or empty: {input_video_p}")
   290→        headless_logger.debug(f"EXTRACT_SPECIFIC_FRAME_FFMPEG: Input video {input_video_p} not found or empty. Returning False.")
   291→        return False
   292→
   293→    cmd = [
   294→        'ffmpeg',
   295→        '-y',  # Overwrite output without asking
   296→        '-i', str(input_video_p.resolve()),
   297→        '-vf', f"select=eq(n\\,{frame_number})", # Escaped comma for ffmpeg filter syntax
   298→        '-vframes', '1',
   299→        str(output_image_p.resolve())
   300→    ]
   301→
   302→    headless_logger.debug(f"EXTRACT_SPECIFIC_FRAME_FFMPEG: Running command: {' '.join(cmd)}")
   303→    try:
   304→        process = subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, capture_output=True, text=True, encoding='utf-8')
   305→        headless_logger.debug(f"EXTRACT_SPECIFIC_FRAME_FFMPEG: Successfully extracted frame {frame_number} to {output_image_p}")
   306→        if process.stderr:
   307→            headless_logger.debug(f"FFmpeg stderr (for frame extraction to {output_image_p}):\n{process.stderr}")
   308→        if not output_image_p.exists() or output_image_p.stat().st_size == 0:
   309→            headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg command for frame extraction to {output_image_p} apparently succeeded but output file is missing or empty.")
   310→            headless_logger.debug(f"FFmpeg command for {output_image_p} (frame extraction) produced no output. stdout:\n{process.stdout}\nstderr:\n{process.stderr}")
   311→            return False
   312→        return True
   313→    except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
   314→        headless_logger.error(f"Error during FFmpeg frame extraction for {output_image_p}:")
   315→        headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg command: {' '.join(e.cmd)}")
   316→        if e.stdout: headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg stdout:\n{e.stdout}")
   317→        if e.stderr: headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg stderr:\n{e.stderr}")
   318→        headless_logger.debug(f"FFmpeg frame extraction failed for {output_image_p}. Error: {e}")
   319→        if output_image_p.exists(): output_image_p.unlink(missing_ok=True)
   320→        return False
   321→    except FileNotFoundError:
   322→        headless_logger.error("ffmpeg command not found. Please ensure ffmpeg is installed and in your PATH.")
   323→        headless_logger.debug("FFmpeg command not found during frame extraction.")
   324→        raise

   326→# --- FFMPEG-based video concatenation (alternative to stitch_videos_ffmpeg if caller manages temp dir) ---
   327→def concatenate_videos_ffmpeg(
   328→    video_paths: list[str | Path],
   329→    output_path: str | Path,
   330→    temp_dir_for_list: str | Path # Directory where the list file will be created
   331→):
   332→    """Concatenates multiple video files into one using FFmpeg, using a provided temp directory for the list file."""
   333→    output_p = Path(output_path)
   334→    output_p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   335→    temp_dir_p = Path(temp_dir_for_list)
   336→    temp_dir_p.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   337→
   338→    if not video_paths:
   339→        headless_logger.warning("No videos to concatenate.")
   340→        headless_logger.debug("CONCATENATE_VIDEOS_FFMPEG: No video paths provided. Returning.")
   341→        if output_p.exists(): output_p.unlink(missing_ok=True)
   342→        return
   343→
   344→    valid_video_paths = []
   345→    for p_item in video_paths:
   346→        resolved_p_item = Path(p_item).resolve()
   347→        if resolved_p_item.exists() and resolved_p_item.stat().st_size > 0:
   348→            valid_video_paths.append(resolved_p_item)
   349→        else:
   350→            headless_logger.warning(f"Video segment {resolved_p_item} for concatenation is missing or empty. Skipping.")
   351→            headless_logger.debug(f"CONCATENATE_VIDEOS_FFMPEG: Skipping invalid video segment {resolved_p_item}")
   352→
   353→    if not valid_video_paths:
   354→        headless_logger.warning("No valid video segments found to concatenate after checks.")
   355→        headless_logger.debug("CONCATENATE_VIDEOS_FFMPEG: No valid video segments. Returning.")
   356→        if output_p.exists(): output_p.unlink(missing_ok=True)
   357→        return
   358→
   359→    filelist_path = temp_dir_p / "ffmpeg_concat_filelist.txt"
   360→    with open(filelist_path, 'w', encoding='utf-8') as f:
   361→        for video_path_item in valid_video_paths:
   362→            f.write(f"file '{video_path_item.as_posix()}'\n") # Use as_posix() for ffmpeg list file
   363→
   364→    cmd = [
   365→        'ffmpeg', '-y',
   366→        '-f', 'concat',
   367→        '-safe', '0',
   368→        '-i', str(filelist_path.resolve()),
   369→        '-c', 'copy',
   370→        str(output_p.resolve())
   371→    ]
   372→
   373→    headless_logger.debug(f"CONCATENATE_VIDEOS_FFMPEG: Running command: {' '.join(cmd)} with list file {filelist_path}")
   374→    try:
   375→        process = subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, capture_output=True, text=True, encoding='utf-8')
   376→        headless_logger.essential(f"Successfully concatenated videos into: {output_p}")
   377→        headless_logger.debug(f"CONCATENATE_VIDEOS_FFMPEG: Success. Output: {output_p}")
   378→        if process.stderr:
   379→            headless_logger.debug(f"FFmpeg stderr (for concatenation to {output_p}):\n{process.stderr}")
   380→        if not output_p.exists() or output_p.stat().st_size == 0:
   381→             headless_logger.warning(f"FFmpeg concatenation to {output_p} apparently succeeded but output file is missing or empty.")
   382→             headless_logger.debug(f"FFmpeg command for {output_p} (concatenation) produced no output. stdout:\n{process.stdout}\nstderr:\n{process.stderr}")
   383→    except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
   384→        headless_logger.error(f"Error during FFmpeg concatenation for {output_p}:")
   385→        headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg command: {' '.join(e.cmd)}")
   386→        if e.stdout: headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg stdout:\n{e.stdout}")
   387→        if e.stderr: headless_logger.error(f"FFmpeg stderr:\n{e.stderr}")
   388→        headless_logger.debug(f"FFmpeg concatenation failed for {output_p}. Error: {e}")
   389→        if output_p.exists(): output_p.unlink(missing_ok=True)
   390→        raise
   391→    except FileNotFoundError:
   392→        headless_logger.error("ffmpeg command not found. Please ensure ffmpeg is installed and in your PATH.")
   393→        headless_logger.debug("CONCATENATE_VIDEOS_FFMPEG: ffmpeg command not found.")
   394→        raise
   395→
   396→# --- Video properties extraction with ffprobe fallback ---
   397→def _get_video_props_ffprobe(video_path_str: str) -> tuple[int | None, float | None]:
   398→    """
   399→    Fallback method using ffprobe to get frame count and FPS.
   400→    More reliable for WebM and other containers where OpenCV struggles.
   401→    """
   402→    import subprocess
   403→    try:
   404→        # Get frame count and fps using ffprobe
   405→        # For containers without frame count in metadata, we use nb_read_frames (requires counting)
   406→        # First try fast method: nb_frames from stream metadata
   407→        probe_cmd = [
   408→            'ffprobe', '-v', 'error',
   409→            '-select_streams', 'v:0',
   410→            '-show_entries', 'stream=nb_frames,r_frame_rate,duration',
   411→            '-of', 'json',
   412→            video_path_str
   413→        ]
   414→        result = subprocess.run(probe_cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=30)
   415→        if result.returncode != 0:
   416→            headless_logger.debug(f"GET_VIDEO_PROPS_FFPROBE: ffprobe failed: {result.stderr}")
   417→            return None, None
   418→
   419→        import json
   420→        data = json.loads(result.stdout)
   421→        streams = data.get('streams', [])
   422→        if not streams:
   423→            return None, None
   424→
   425→        stream = streams[0]
   426→
   427→        # Parse FPS from r_frame_rate (e.g., "30/1" or "30000/1001")
   428→        fps = None
   429→        r_frame_rate = stream.get('r_frame_rate', '')
   430→        if r_frame_rate and '/' in r_frame_rate:
   431→            num, den = r_frame_rate.split('/')
   432→            if float(den) > 0:
   433→                fps = float(num) / float(den)
   434→
   435→        # Try to get frame count from metadata
   436→        frame_count = None
   437→        nb_frames = stream.get('nb_frames')
   438→        if nb_frames and nb_frames != 'N/A':
   439→            try:
   440→                frame_count = int(nb_frames)
   441→            except (ValueError, TypeError):
   442→                pass
   443→
   444→        # If no frame count in metadata, estimate from duration * fps
   445→        # This is common for WebM files
   446→        if frame_count is None and fps and fps > 0:
   447→            duration = stream.get('duration')
   448→            if duration and duration != 'N/A':
   449→                try:
   450→                    frame_count = int(float(duration) * fps)
   451→                    headless_logger.debug(f"GET_VIDEO_PROPS_FFPROBE: Estimated frame count from duration: {frame_count}")
   452→                except (ValueError, TypeError):
   453→                    pass
   454→
   455→        # Last resort: actually count frames (slow but accurate)
   456→        if frame_count is None:
   457→            headless_logger.debug(f"GET_VIDEO_PROPS_FFPROBE: Counting frames for {video_path_str} (may be slow)...")
   458→            count_cmd = [
   459→                'ffprobe', '-v', 'error',
   460→                '-select_streams', 'v:0',
   461→                '-count_frames',
   462→                '-show_entries', 'stream=nb_read_frames',
   463→                '-of', 'csv=p=0',
   464→                video_path_str
   465→            ]
   466→            count_result = subprocess.run(count_cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=120)
   467→            if count_result.returncode == 0 and count_result.stdout.strip():
   468→                try:
   469→                    frame_count = int(count_result.stdout.strip())
   470→                except (ValueError, TypeError):
   471→                    pass
   472→
   473→        headless_logger.debug(f"GET_VIDEO_PROPS_FFPROBE: {video_path_str} - Frames: {frame_count}, FPS: {fps}")
   474→        return frame_count, fps
   475→
   476→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   477→        headless_logger.debug(f"GET_VIDEO_PROPS_FFPROBE: Timeout processing {video_path_str}")
   478→        return None, None
   479→    except (subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError, ValueError) as e:
   480→        headless_logger.debug(f"GET_VIDEO_PROPS_FFPROBE: Exception processing {video_path_str}: {e}")
   481→        return None, None
   482→
   483→def get_video_frame_count_and_fps(input_video_path: str | Path) -> tuple[int | None, float | None]:
   484→    """
   485→    Gets frame count and FPS of a video.
   486→
   487→    Uses OpenCV first (fast), then falls back to ffprobe for containers
   488→    where OpenCV is unreliable (e.g., WebM with VP8/VP9 codec).
   489→
   490→    Returns (None, None) on failure.
   491→    """
   492→    video_path_str = str(Path(input_video_path).resolve())
   493→    cap = None
   494→    try:
   495→        cap = cv2.VideoCapture(video_path_str)
   496→        if not cap.isOpened():
   497→            headless_logger.debug(f"GET_VIDEO_FRAME_COUNT_FPS: Could not open video with OpenCV: {video_path_str}")
   498→            # Try ffprobe as fallback
   499→            return _get_video_props_ffprobe(video_path_str)
   500→
   501→        frame_count = int(cap.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_COUNT))
   502→        fps = cap.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FPS)
   503→
   504→        # Validate frame_count and fps as they can sometimes be 0 or negative for problematic files/streams
   505→        valid_frame_count = frame_count if frame_count > 0 else None
   506→        valid_fps = fps if fps > 0 else None
   507→
   508→        # If OpenCV failed to get frame count (common for WebM), try ffprobe
   509→        if valid_frame_count is None:
   510→            headless_logger.debug(f"GET_VIDEO_FRAME_COUNT_FPS: OpenCV reported non-positive frame count ({frame_count}) for {video_path_str}. Trying ffprobe...")
   511→            ffprobe_frame_count, ffprobe_fps = _get_video_props_ffprobe(video_path_str)
   512→            if ffprobe_frame_count is not None:
   513→                valid_frame_count = ffprobe_frame_count
   514→            if valid_fps is None and ffprobe_fps is not None:
   515→                valid_fps = ffprobe_fps
   516→
   517→        if valid_fps is None:
   518→            headless_logger.debug(f"GET_VIDEO_FRAME_COUNT_FPS: Video {video_path_str} reported non-positive FPS: {fps}. Treating as unknown.")
   519→
   520→        headless_logger.debug(f"GET_VIDEO_FRAME_COUNT_FPS: Video {video_path_str} - Frames: {valid_frame_count}, FPS: {valid_fps}")
   521→        return valid_frame_count, valid_fps
   522→    except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
   523→        headless_logger.debug(f"GET_VIDEO_FRAME_COUNT_FPS: Exception processing {video_path_str}: {e}")
   524→        # Try ffprobe as fallback on any exception
   525→        return _get_video_props_ffprobe(video_path_str)
   526→    finally:
   527→        if cap:
   528→            cap.release()
   529→
   530→def image_to_frame(image_path_str: str | Path, target_resolution_wh: tuple[int, int] | None = None, task_id_for_logging: str | None = "generic_task", image_download_dir: Path | str | None = None, debug_mode: bool = False) -> np.ndarray | None:
   531→    """
   532→    Load an image, optionally resize, and convert to BGR NumPy array.
   533→    If image_path_str is a URL and image_download_dir is provided, it attempts to download it first.
   534→    """
   535→    resolved_image_path_str = image_path_str # Default to original path
   536→
   537→    if isinstance(image_path_str, str): # Only attempt download if it's a string (potentially a URL)
   538→        resolved_image_path_str = download_image_if_url(image_path_str, image_download_dir, task_id_for_logging, debug_mode)
   539→
   540→    image_path = Path(resolved_image_path_str)
   541→
   542→    if not image_path.exists():
   543→        headless_logger.debug(f"Task {task_id_for_logging}: Image file not found at {image_path} (original input: {image_path_str}).")
   544→        return None
   545→    try:
   546→        img = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB") # Ensure RGB for consistent processing
   547→        if target_resolution_wh:
   548→            img = img.resize(target_resolution_wh, Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)
   549→        return cv2.cvtColor(np.array(img), cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR)
   550→    except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
   551→        headless_logger.debug(f"Error loading image {image_path} (original input {image_path_str}): {e}")
   552→        return None
   553→
   554→def apply_strength_to_image(
   555→    image_path_input: Path | str, # Changed name to avoid confusion
   556→    strength: float,
   557→    output_path: Path,
   558→    target_resolution_wh: tuple[int, int] | None,
   559→    task_id_for_logging: str | None = "generic_task",
   560→    image_download_dir: Path | str | None = None
   561→) -> Path | None:
   562→    """
   563→    Applies a brightness adjustment (strength) to an image, optionally resizes, and saves it.
   564→    If image_path_input is a URL string and image_download_dir is provided, it attempts to download it first.
   565→    """
   566→    resolved_image_path_str = str(image_path_input)
   567→
   568→    if isinstance(image_path_input, str):
   569→         resolved_image_path_str = download_image_if_url(image_path_input, image_download_dir, task_id_for_logging)
   570→    # Check if image_path_input was a Path object representing a URL (less common for this function)
   571→    elif isinstance(image_path_input, Path) and image_path_input.as_posix().startswith(('http://', 'https://')):
   572→         resolved_image_path_str = download_image_if_url(image_path_input.as_posix(), image_download_dir, task_id_for_logging)
   573→
   574→    actual_image_path = Path(resolved_image_path_str)
   575→
   576→    if not actual_image_path.exists():
   577→        headless_logger.debug(f"Task {task_id_for_logging}: Source image not found at {actual_image_path} (original input: {image_path_input}) for strength application.")
   578→        return None
   579→    try:
   580→        # Open the potentially downloaded or original local image
   581→        img = Image.open(actual_image_path).convert("RGB") # Ensure RGB
   582→
   583→        if target_resolution_wh:
   584→            img = img.resize(target_resolution_wh, Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)
   585→
   586→        # Apply the strength factor using PIL.ImageEnhance for brightness
   587→        enhancer = ImageEnhance.Brightness(img)
   588→        processed_img = enhancer.enhance(strength) # 'strength' is the factor for brightness
   589→
   590→        # Save the adjusted image
   591→        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   592→        processed_img.save(output_path) # Save PIL image directly
   593→
   594→        headless_logger.debug(f"Task {task_id_for_logging}: Applied strength {strength} to {actual_image_path.name}, saved to {output_path.name} with resolution {target_resolution_wh if target_resolution_wh else 'original'}")
   595→        return output_path
   596→    except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
   597→        headless_logger.debug(f"Task {task_id_for_logging}: Error in _apply_strength_to_image for {actual_image_path}: {e}")
   598→        traceback.print_exc()
   599→        return None
   600→
   601→def _copy_to_folder_with_unique_name(source_path: Path, target_dir: Path, base_name: str, extension: str) -> Path | None:
   602→    """Copies a file to a target directory with a unique name based on timestamp and random string."""
   603→    import shutil
   604→
   605→    if not source_path:
   606→        headless_logger.debug(f"COPY: Source path is None for {base_name}{extension}. Skipping copy.")
   607→        return None
   608→
   609→    source_path_obj = Path(source_path)
   610→    if not source_path_obj.exists():
   611→        headless_logger.debug(f"COPY: Source file {source_path_obj} does not exist. Skipping copy.")
   612→        return None
   613→
   614→    # Sanitize extension for _get_unique_target_path
   615→    actual_extension = source_path_obj.suffix if source_path_obj.suffix else extension
   616→    if not actual_extension.startswith('.'):
   617→        actual_extension = '.' + actual_extension
   618→
   619→    # Determine unique target path using the new helper
   620→    from source.utils.download_utils import _get_unique_target_path
   621→    target_file = _get_unique_target_path(target_dir, base_name, actual_extension)
   622→
   623→    try:
   624→        # target_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True) # _get_unique_target_path handles this
   625→        shutil.copy2(str(source_path_obj), str(target_file))
   626→        headless_logger.debug(f"COPY: Copied {source_path_obj.name} to {target_file}")
   627→        return target_file # Return the path of the copied file
   628→    except OSError as e_copy:
   629→        headless_logger.debug(f"COPY: Failed to copy {source_path_obj} to {target_file}: {e_copy}")
   630→        return None
   631→
   632→def get_image_dimensions_pil(image_path: str | Path) -> tuple[int, int]:
   633→    """Returns the dimensions of an image file as (width, height)."""
   634→    with Image.open(image_path) as img:
   635→        return img.size
   636→
   637→# Added to adjust the brightness of an image/frame.
   638→def adjust_frame_brightness(frame: np.ndarray, factor: float) -> np.ndarray:
   639→    """Adjusts the brightness of a given frame.
   640→    The 'factor' is interpreted as a delta from the CLI argument:
   641→    - Positive factor (e.g., 0.1) makes it darker (target_alpha = 1.0 - 0.1 = 0.9).
   642→    - Negative factor (e.g., -0.1) makes it brighter (target_alpha = 1.0 - (-0.1) = 1.1).
   643→    - Zero factor means no change (target_alpha = 1.0).
   644→    """
   645→    # Convert the CLI-style factor to an alpha for cv2.convertScaleAbs
   646→    # CLI factor: positive = darker, negative = brighter
   647→    # cv2 alpha: >1 = brighter, <1 = darker
   648→    cv2_alpha = 1.0 - factor
   649→    return cv2.convertScaleAbs(frame, alpha=cv2_alpha, beta=0)
   650→
   651→def get_sequential_target_path(target_dir: Path, name_stem: str, suffix: str) -> Path:
   652→    """Generates a unique target Path in the given directory by appending a number if needed."""
   653→    if not suffix.startswith('.'):
   654→        suffix = f".{suffix}"
   655→
   656→    final_path = target_dir / f"{name_stem}{suffix}"
   657→    counter = 1
   658→    while final_path.exists():
   659→        final_path = target_dir / f"{name_stem}_{counter}{suffix}"
   660→        counter += 1
   661→    return final_path
   662→
   663→def load_pil_images(
   664→    paths_list_or_str: list[str] | str,
   665→    wgp_convert_func: callable,
   666→    image_download_dir: Path | str | None,
   667→    task_id_for_log: str) -> list | None:
   668→    """
   669→    Loads one or more images from paths or URLs, downloads them if necessary,
   670→    and applies a conversion function.
   671→    """
   672→    if not paths_list_or_str:
   673→        return None
   674→
   675→    paths_list = paths_list_or_str if isinstance(paths_list_or_str, list) else [paths_list_or_str]
   676→    images = []
   677→
   678→    for p_str in paths_list:
   679→        local_p_str = download_image_if_url(p_str, image_download_dir, task_id_for_log)
   680→        if not local_p_str:
   681→            headless_logger.debug(f"[Task {task_id_for_log}] Skipping image as download_image_if_url returned nothing for: {p_str}")
   682→            continue
   683→
   684→        p = Path(local_p_str.strip())
   685→        if not p.is_file():
   686→            headless_logger.debug(f"[Task {task_id_for_log}] load_pil_images: Image file not found after potential download: {p} (original: {p_str})")
   687→            continue
   688→        try:
   689→            img = Image.open(p)
   690→            images.append(wgp_convert_func(img))
   691→        except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
   692→            headless_logger.warning(f"Failed to load image {p}: {e}")
   693→
   694→    return images if images else None
   695→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Base classes and utilities for typed parameter handling.
     3→
     4→PRECEDENCE RULES (documented here as single source of truth):
     5→- DB Tasks: top_level > orchestrator_details > orchestrator_payload
     6→- Segments: individual_params > segment_params > orchestrator_payload
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
    10→from typing import Any, Dict, List
    11→import logging
    12→
    13→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    14→
    15→
    16→class ParamGroup(ABC):
    17→    """
    18→    Base class for parameter groups.
    19→    
    20→    Subclasses must implement:
    21→    - from_params(params, **context) -> cls
    22→    - to_wgp_format() -> Dict[str, Any]
    23→    """
    24→    
    25→    @classmethod
    26→    @abstractmethod
    27→    def from_params(cls, params: Dict[str, Any], **context) -> 'ParamGroup':
    28→        """Parse parameters from a dict. Context can include task_id, model, etc."""
    29→        pass
    30→    
    31→    @abstractmethod
    32→    def to_wgp_format(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    33→        """Convert to WGP-compatible format."""
    34→        pass
    35→    
    36→    def validate(self) -> List[str]:
    37→        """Return list of validation errors (empty if valid)."""
    38→        return []
    39→    
    40→    @staticmethod
    41→    def _get_first_of(params: Dict[str, Any], *keys, default=None):
    42→        """Get first non-None value from a list of possible keys."""
    43→        for key in keys:
    44→            if key in params and params[key] is not None:
    45→                return params[key]
    46→        return default
    47→    
    48→    @staticmethod
    49→    def _parse_list(value, separator=',') -> List[str]:
    50→        """Parse a value that could be a list or comma-separated string."""
    51→        if value is None:
    52→            return []
    53→        if isinstance(value, list):
    54→            return value
    55→        if isinstance(value, str):
    56→            return [x.strip() for x in value.split(separator) if x.strip()]
    57→        return [str(value)]
    58→    
    59→    @staticmethod
    60→    def flatten_params(db_params: Dict[str, Any], task_id: str = "") -> Dict[str, Any]:
    61→        """
    62→        Flatten nested DB params with documented precedence.
    63→        
    64→        Precedence: top_level > orchestrator_details > full_orchestrator_payload (legacy)
    65→        """
    66→        result = {}
    67→        
    68→        # Start with full_orchestrator_payload (legacy name, lowest precedence)
    69→        if "full_orchestrator_payload" in db_params and isinstance(db_params["full_orchestrator_payload"], dict):
    70→            result.update(db_params["full_orchestrator_payload"])
    71→        
    72→        # Then orchestrator_details (canonical name, medium precedence)
    73→        if "orchestrator_details" in db_params and isinstance(db_params["orchestrator_details"], dict):
    74→            result.update(db_params["orchestrator_details"])
    75→        
    76→        # Finally top-level params (highest precedence)
    77→        for key, value in db_params.items():
    78→            if key not in ("full_orchestrator_payload", "orchestrator_details"):
    79→                result[key] = value
    80→        
    81→        return result
    82→
    83→
    84→def warn_similar_key(params: Dict[str, Any], expected: str, alternatives: List[str], task_id: str = ""):
    85→    """Log a warning if we find a similar but not exact key."""
    86→    for alt in alternatives:
    87→        if alt in params and expected not in params:
    88→            logger.warning(f"Task {task_id}: Found '{alt}' but expected '{expected}' - using '{alt}'")
    89→
```

```
1→"""
     2→LoRA configuration handling with typed entries.
     3→
     4→Uses LoRAEntry objects instead of parallel arrays to preserve ordering
     5→and ensure filename/multiplier pairs stay in sync.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     9→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Union
    10→from enum import Enum
    11→import os
    12→import logging
    13→
    14→from .base import ParamGroup
    15→
    16→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    17→
    18→
    19→class LoRAStatus(Enum):
    20→    """Status of a LoRA entry."""
    21→    PENDING = "pending"      # URL needs to be downloaded
    22→    DOWNLOADED = "downloaded"  # Downloaded, has local_path
    23→    LOCAL = "local"          # Local file reference
    24→
    25→
    26→@dataclass
    27→class LoRAEntry:
    28→    """
    29→    Single LoRA entry with all its metadata.
    30→    
    31→    Using objects instead of parallel arrays ensures:
    32→    - Filename and multiplier stay paired
    33→    - Order is preserved during operations
    34→    - Clear status tracking
    35→    """
    36→    url: Optional[str] = None
    37→    local_path: Optional[str] = None
    38→    filename: Optional[str] = None
    39→    multiplier: Union[float, str] = 1.0  # str for phase-config format like "1.0;0.5"
    40→    status: LoRAStatus = LoRAStatus.LOCAL
    41→    source: str = ""  # Where this entry came from (for debugging)
    42→    
    43→    def mark_downloaded(self, local_path: str):
    44→        """Mark this entry as downloaded with the given local path."""
    45→        self.local_path = local_path
    46→        self.filename = os.path.basename(local_path)
    47→        self.status = LoRAStatus.DOWNLOADED
    48→    
    49→    def get_effective_path(self) -> Optional[str]:
    50→        """Get the path to use for this LoRA (local_path or filename)."""
    51→        return self.local_path or self.filename
    52→    
    53→    def is_phase_config_multiplier(self) -> bool:
    54→        """Check if multiplier is phase-config format (contains semicolons)."""
    55→        return isinstance(self.multiplier, str) and ';' in str(self.multiplier)
    56→
    57→
    58→@dataclass  
    59→class LoRAConfig(ParamGroup):
    60→    """
    61→    Typed LoRA configuration.
    62→    
    63→    Handles all LoRA naming conventions:
    64→    - WGP: activated_loras, loras_multipliers (string)
    65→    - Internal: lora_names, lora_multipliers (list)
    66→    - Phase config: additional_loras dict with URL keys
    67→    """
    68→    entries: List[LoRAEntry] = field(default_factory=list)
    69→    
    70→    @classmethod
    71→    def from_params(cls, params: Dict[str, Any], **context) -> 'LoRAConfig':
    72→        """
    73→        Parse LoRA config from various input formats.
    74→        
    75→        Handles:
    76→        - activated_loras / lora_names (list of filenames)
    77→        - loras_multipliers / lora_multipliers (string or list)
    78→        - additional_loras (dict of URL -> multiplier for downloads)
    79→        """
    80→        entries = []
    81→        
    82→        # Get filenames from either naming convention
    83→        filenames = (
    84→            params.get('activated_loras') or 
    85→            params.get('lora_names') or 
    86→            []
    87→        )
    88→        if isinstance(filenames, str):
    89→            filenames = [f.strip() for f in filenames.split(',') if f.strip()]
    90→        
    91→        # Get multipliers from either naming convention
    92→        multipliers_raw = (
    93→            params.get('loras_multipliers') or 
    94→            params.get('lora_multipliers') or
    95→            []
    96→        )
    97→        
    98→        # Parse multipliers
    99→        if isinstance(multipliers_raw, str):
   100→            # Could be space-separated or comma-separated
   101→            if ';' in multipliers_raw:
   102→                # Phase-config format: space-separated strings with semicolons
   103→                multipliers = multipliers_raw.split()
   104→            elif ',' in multipliers_raw:
   105→                multipliers = [m.strip() for m in multipliers_raw.split(',')]
   106→            else:
   107→                multipliers = multipliers_raw.split()
   108→        elif isinstance(multipliers_raw, list):
   109→            multipliers = multipliers_raw
   110→        else:
   111→            multipliers = []
   112→        
   113→        # Create entries from filenames (could be URLs, absolute paths, or filenames)
   114→        for i, filename in enumerate(filenames):
   115→            mult = multipliers[i] if i < len(multipliers) else 1.0
   116→            # Try to convert to float if it's a simple number
   117→            if isinstance(mult, str) and ';' not in mult:
   118→                try:
   119→                    mult = float(mult)
   120→                except ValueError:
   121→                    pass
   122→            
   123→            # Detect if this is a URL that needs downloading
   124→            is_url = filename.startswith(('http://', 'https://'))
   125→            
   126→            if is_url:
   127→                # URL needs downloading
   128→                entries.append(LoRAEntry(
   129→                    url=filename,
   130→                    filename=os.path.basename(filename),
   131→                    multiplier=mult,
   132→                    status=LoRAStatus.PENDING,
   133→                    source='params_url'
   134→                ))
   135→            else:
   136→                # Local file or filename
   137→                entries.append(LoRAEntry(
   138→                    filename=filename,
   139→                    local_path=filename if os.path.isabs(filename) else None,
   140→                    multiplier=mult,
   141→                    status=LoRAStatus.LOCAL,
   142→                    source='params'
   143→                ))
   144→        
   145→        # Handle additional_loras (URLs needing download)
   146→        additional_loras = params.get('additional_loras', {})
   147→        if isinstance(additional_loras, dict):
   148→            for url, mult in additional_loras.items():
   149→                # Check if we already have this URL's file
   150→                filename = os.path.basename(url) if url else None
   151→                existing = next((e for e in entries if e.filename == filename), None)
   152→                
   153→                if existing:
   154→                    # Update existing entry with URL info
   155→                    existing.url = url
   156→                    if mult and ';' in str(mult):
   157→                        # Phase-config multiplier takes precedence
   158→                        existing.multiplier = mult
   159→                    existing.status = LoRAStatus.PENDING
   160→                else:
   161→                    # New entry for download
   162→                    entries.append(LoRAEntry(
   163→                        url=url,
   164→                        filename=filename,
   165→                        multiplier=mult if mult else 1.0,
   166→                        status=LoRAStatus.PENDING,
   167→                        source='additional_loras'
   168→                    ))
   169→        
   170→        return cls(entries=entries)
   171→    
   172→    @classmethod
   173→    def from_phase_config(cls, phase_config: Dict[str, Any], **context) -> 'LoRAConfig':
   174→        """
   175→        Parse LoRA config from phase_config structure.
   176→
   177→        Phase config has LoRAs per phase with per-phase multipliers.
   178→        We combine into single entries with semicolon-separated multipliers.
   179→        """
   180→        entries = []
   181→        phases = phase_config.get('phases', [])
   182→
   183→        # Collect all unique LoRAs with their per-phase multipliers
   184→        lora_phases: Dict[str, List[float]] = {}  # url -> [mult_phase1, mult_phase2, ...]
   185→
   186→        for phase_idx, phase in enumerate(phases):
   187→            phase_loras = phase.get('loras', [])
   188→            for lora in phase_loras:
   189→                url = lora.get('url', '')
   190→                mult = lora.get('multiplier', 1.0)
   191→
   192→                if url not in lora_phases:
   193→                    lora_phases[url] = [0.0] * len(phases)
   194→                lora_phases[url][phase_idx] = mult
   195→
   196→        # Create entries with combined multipliers
   197→        for url, mults in lora_phases.items():
   198→            mult_str = ';'.join(str(m) for m in mults)
   199→            entries.append(LoRAEntry(
   200→                url=url,
   201→                filename=os.path.basename(url) if url else None,
   202→                multiplier=mult_str,
   203→                status=LoRAStatus.PENDING,
   204→                source='phase_config'
   205→            ))
   206→
   207→        return cls(entries=entries)
   208→
   209→    @classmethod
   210→    def from_segment_loras(cls, segment_loras: List[Dict[str, Any]], **context) -> 'LoRAConfig':
   211→        """
   212→        Parse LoRA config from per-segment LoRA override format.
   213→
   214→        Args:
   215→            segment_loras: List of LoRA dicts from frontend:
   216→                [{"id": "...", "path": "...", "strength": 0.8, "name": "..."}, ...]
   217→
   218→        Returns:
   219→            LoRAConfig with entries for each LoRA
   220→        """
   221→        entries = []
   222→
   223→        for lora_dict in segment_loras:
   224→            # Frontend sends: {id, path, strength, name?}
   225→            # path could be a URL or a local path/filename
   226→            lora_path = lora_dict.get('path', '')
   227→            strength = lora_dict.get('strength', 1.0)
   228→            if not lora_path:
   229→                continue
   230→
   231→            # Detect if this is a URL that needs downloading
   232→            is_url = lora_path.startswith(('http://', 'https://'))
   233→
   234→            if is_url:
   235→                # URL needs downloading
   236→                entries.append(LoRAEntry(
   237→                    url=lora_path,
   238→                    filename=os.path.basename(lora_path),
   239→                    multiplier=strength,
   240→                    status=LoRAStatus.PENDING,
   241→                    source='segment_loras'
   242→                ))
   243→            else:
   244→                # Local file or filename
   245→                entries.append(LoRAEntry(
   246→                    filename=lora_path,
   247→                    local_path=lora_path if os.path.isabs(lora_path) else None,
   248→                    multiplier=strength,
   249→                    status=LoRAStatus.LOCAL,
   250→                    source='segment_loras'
   251→                ))
   252→
   253→        return cls(entries=entries)
   254→    
   255→    def to_wgp_format(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   256→        """
   257→        Convert to WGP-compatible format.
   258→        
   259→        Returns:
   260→            activated_loras: list of filenames/paths
   261→            loras_multipliers: space-separated string (for phase-config) or comma-separated
   262→        """
   263→        if not self.entries:
   264→            return {'activated_loras': [], 'loras_multipliers': ''}
   265→        
   266→        # Only include downloaded/local entries
   267→        ready_entries = [e for e in self.entries if e.status != LoRAStatus.PENDING]
   268→        
   269→        activated_loras = []
   270→        multipliers = []
   271→        
   272→        for entry in ready_entries:
   273→            path = entry.get_effective_path()
   274→            if path:
   275→                activated_loras.append(path)
   276→                multipliers.append(str(entry.multiplier))
   277→        
   278→        # Format multipliers as space-separated string
   279→        # WGP's preparse_loras_multipliers() splits on spaces, so both
   280→        # phase-config ("0.9;0.5 1.1;1.1") and regular ("0.9 1.1 0.5")
   281→        # must be space-separated
   282→        loras_multipliers = ' '.join(multipliers)
   283→        
   284→        return {
   285→            'activated_loras': activated_loras,
   286→            'loras_multipliers': loras_multipliers
   287→        }
   288→    
   289→    def has_pending_downloads(self) -> bool:
   290→        """Check if any entries need downloading."""
   291→        return any(e.status == LoRAStatus.PENDING and e.url for e in self.entries)
   292→    
   293→    def get_pending_downloads(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   294→        """Get dict of URL -> multiplier for pending downloads."""
   295→        return {
   296→            e.url: e.multiplier 
   297→            for e in self.entries 
   298→            if e.status == LoRAStatus.PENDING and e.url
   299→        }
   300→    
   301→    def mark_downloaded(self, url: str, local_path: str):
   302→        """Mark a URL as downloaded with the given local path."""
   303→        for entry in self.entries:
   304→            if entry.url == url:
   305→                entry.mark_downloaded(local_path)
   306→                return
   307→    
   308→    def merge(self, other: 'LoRAConfig') -> 'LoRAConfig':
   309→        """
   310→        Merge another LoRAConfig, with other taking precedence for duplicates.
   311→        Phase-config multipliers take precedence over regular ones.
   312→        """
   313→        merged_entries = list(self.entries)
   314→        
   315→        for other_entry in other.entries:
   316→            # Find existing entry by filename
   317→            existing_idx = next(
   318→                (i for i, e in enumerate(merged_entries) 
   319→                 if e.filename == other_entry.filename),
   320→                None
   321→            )
   322→            
   323→            if existing_idx is not None:
   324→                existing = merged_entries[existing_idx]
   325→                # Phase-config multipliers take precedence
   326→                if other_entry.is_phase_config_multiplier():
   327→                    merged_entries[existing_idx] = other_entry
   328→                elif not existing.is_phase_config_multiplier():
   329→                    # Other takes precedence for non-phase-config
   330→                    merged_entries[existing_idx] = other_entry
   331→            else:
   332→                merged_entries.append(other_entry)
   333→        
   334→        return LoRAConfig(entries=merged_entries)
   335→    
   336→    def validate(self) -> List[str]:
   337→        """Validate the LoRA configuration."""
   338→        errors = []
   339→        
   340→        for entry in self.entries:
   341→            if entry.status == LoRAStatus.LOCAL and entry.filename:
   342→                # Could add file existence check here if needed
   343→                pass
   344→            if entry.status == LoRAStatus.PENDING and not entry.url:
   345→                errors.append(f"Pending LoRA entry has no URL: {entry.filename}")
   346→        
   347→        return errors
   348→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Phase configuration handling.
     3→
     4→Defines dataclasses for phase-based generation config (PhaseConfig) and a
     5→parser (from_params) that delegates to parse_phase_config() for the complex
     6→timestep/threshold calculation logic.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    10→from typing import Any, Dict, Optional
    11→import logging
    12→
    13→from .base import ParamGroup
    14→
    15→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    16→
    17→
    18→@dataclass
    19→class PhaseConfig(ParamGroup):
    20→    """Phase-based generation configuration with typed field access."""
    21→    raw_config: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    22→    parsed_output: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    23→    
    24→    # Key parsed values (for typed access)
    25→    num_phases: int = 0
    26→    total_steps: int = 0
    27→    model_name: Optional[str] = None
    28→    
    29→    # For model patching
    30→    patch_config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
    31→    patch_model_name: Optional[str] = None
    32→    
    33→    @classmethod
    34→    def from_params(cls, params: Dict[str, Any], **context) -> 'PhaseConfig':
    35→        """Parse phase config by delegating to parse_phase_config()."""
    36→        phase_config = params.get('phase_config')
    37→        if not phase_config:
    38→            return cls()
    39→        
    40→        task_id = context.get('task_id', '')
    41→        model_name = context.get('model') or params.get('model_name')
    42→        debug_mode = context.get('debug_mode', False)
    43→        
    44→        # Calculate total steps from steps_per_phase
    45→        steps_per_phase = phase_config.get('steps_per_phase', [2, 2, 2])
    46→        total_steps = sum(steps_per_phase)
    47→        
    48→        try:
    49→            from source.core.params.phase_config_parser import parse_phase_config
    50→
    51→            parsed = parse_phase_config(
    52→                phase_config=phase_config,
    53→                num_inference_steps=total_steps,
    54→                task_id=task_id,
    55→                model_name=model_name,
    56→                debug_mode=debug_mode
    57→            )
    58→            
    59→            # Extract patch config if present
    60→            patch_config = parsed.pop('_patch_config', None)
    61→            
    62→            return cls(
    63→                raw_config=phase_config,
    64→                parsed_output=parsed,
    65→                num_phases=len(steps_per_phase),
    66→                total_steps=total_steps,
    67→                model_name=model_name,
    68→                patch_config=patch_config,
    69→                patch_model_name=model_name,
    70→            )
    71→            
    72→        except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as e:
    73→            logger.error(f"Task {task_id}: Failed to parse phase_config: {e}")
    74→            return cls(raw_config=phase_config)
    75→    
    76→    def is_empty(self) -> bool:
    77→        """Check if this is an empty/unset phase config."""
    78→        return not self.raw_config
    79→    
    80→    def has_patch_config(self) -> bool:
    81→        """Check if this phase config requires model patching."""
    82→        return self.patch_config is not None
    83→    
    84→    def to_wgp_format(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    85→        """
    86→        Convert to WGP-compatible format.
    87→        
    88→        Returns the parsed_output minus internal keys.
    89→        """
    90→        if not self.parsed_output:
    91→            return {}
    92→        
    93→        result = {}
    94→        
    95→        # Copy all parsed values except internal ones
    96→        internal_keys = {'_patch_config', '_parsed_phase_config', '_phase_config_model_name'}
    97→        for key, value in self.parsed_output.items():
    98→            if key not in internal_keys and value is not None:
    99→                result[key] = value
   100→        
   101→        return result
   102→    
   103→    def get_lora_info(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   104→        """
   105→        Get LoRA-related info from parsed phase config.
   106→        
   107→        Returns dict with lora_names, lora_multipliers, additional_loras.
   108→        """
   109→        return {
   110→            'lora_names': self.parsed_output.get('lora_names', []),
   111→            'lora_multipliers': self.parsed_output.get('lora_multipliers', []),
   112→            'additional_loras': self.parsed_output.get('additional_loras', {}),
   113→        }
   114→    
   115→    def validate(self) -> list:
   116→        """Validate phase configuration."""
   117→        errors = []
   118→        
   119→        if self.raw_config and not self.parsed_output:
   120→            errors.append("Phase config present but failed to parse")
   121→        
   122→        return errors
   123→
```

```
1→"""LoRA file validation, normalization, and special settings helpers."""
     2→
     3→from pathlib import Path
     4→
     5→from source.core.log import headless_logger
     6→
     7→__all__ = [
     8→    "LORA_FULL_MODEL_TENSOR_THRESHOLD",
     9→    "LORA_EXCESSIVE_TENSOR_THRESHOLD",
    10→    "HTML_SNIFF_BYTES",
    11→    "validate_lora_file",
    12→    "check_loras_in_directory",
    13→]
    14→
    15→# --- LoRA validation thresholds ---
    16→# A safetensors file with more tensor keys than this is likely a full model checkpoint, not a LoRA.
    17→LORA_FULL_MODEL_TENSOR_THRESHOLD = 100
    18→# A safetensors file with more tensor keys than this is suspiciously large even for a full model.
    19→LORA_EXCESSIVE_TENSOR_THRESHOLD = 10_000
    20→# Number of bytes to read when sniffing a file header for HTML error pages.
    21→HTML_SNIFF_BYTES = 1024
    22→
    23→def validate_lora_file(file_path: Path, filename: str) -> tuple[bool, str]:
    24→    """
    25→    Validates a LoRA file for size and format integrity.
    26→
    27→    Returns:
    28→        (is_valid, error_message)
    29→    """
    30→    if not file_path.exists():
    31→        return False, f"File does not exist: {file_path}"
    32→
    33→    file_size = file_path.stat().st_size
    34→
    35→    # Known LoRA size ranges (in bytes)
    36→    # These are based on common LoRA architectures and rank sizes
    37→    LORA_SIZE_RANGES = {
    38→        # Very small LoRAs (rank 4-8)
    39→        'tiny': (1_000_000, 50_000_000),      # 1MB - 50MB
    40→        # Standard LoRAs (rank 16-32)
    41→        'standard': (50_000_000, 500_000_000),  # 50MB - 500MB
    42→        # Large LoRAs (rank 64+) or full model fine-tunes
    43→        'large': (500_000_000, 5_000_000_000), # 500MB - 5GB
    44→        # Extremely large (full model weights)
    45→        'xlarge': (5_000_000_000, 50_000_000_000)  # 5GB - 50GB
    46→    }
    47→
    48→    # Check if file size is within any reasonable range
    49→    in_valid_range = any(
    50→        min_size <= file_size <= max_size
    51→        for min_size, max_size in LORA_SIZE_RANGES.values()
    52→    )
    53→
    54→    if not in_valid_range:
    55→        if file_size < 1_000_000:  # Less than 1MB
    56→            return False, f"File too small ({file_size:,} bytes) - likely corrupted or incomplete download"
    57→        elif file_size > 50_000_000_000:  # More than 50GB
    58→            return False, f"File too large ({file_size:,} bytes) - likely not a LoRA file"
    59→
    60→    # For safetensors files, try to open and inspect
    61→    if filename.endswith('.safetensors'):
    62→        try:
    63→            import safetensors.torch as st
    64→            with st.safe_open(file_path, framework="pt") as f:
    65→                keys = list(f.keys())
    66→
    67→                # LoRAs typically have keys like "lora_down.weight", "lora_up.weight", etc.
    68→                lora_indicators = ['lora_down', 'lora_up', 'lora.down', 'lora.up', 'lora_A', 'lora_B']
    69→                has_lora_keys = any(indicator in key for key in keys for indicator in lora_indicators)
    70→
    71→                if not has_lora_keys and len(keys) > LORA_FULL_MODEL_TENSOR_THRESHOLD:
    72→                    # Might be a full model checkpoint rather than a LoRA
    73→                    headless_logger.warning(f"{filename} appears to be a full model checkpoint ({len(keys)} tensors) rather than a LoRA")
    74→                elif not has_lora_keys:
    75→                    headless_logger.warning(f"{filename} doesn't appear to contain LoRA weights (no lora_* keys found)")
    76→
    77→                # Check for reasonable number of parameters
    78→                if len(keys) == 0:
    79→                    return False, "Safetensors file contains no tensors"
    80→                elif len(keys) > LORA_EXCESSIVE_TENSOR_THRESHOLD:
    81→                    headless_logger.warning(f"{filename} contains many tensors ({len(keys)}) - might be a full model")
    82→
    83→        except ImportError:
    84→            headless_logger.debug(f"[WARNING] safetensors not available for detailed validation of {filename}")
    85→        except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
    86→            return False, f"Safetensors file appears corrupted: {e}"
    87→
    88→    # Additional checks for common corruption patterns
    89→    if file_size == 0:
    90→        return False, "File is empty"
    91→
    92→    # For binary files, check they don't start with common error HTML patterns
    93→    try:
    94→        with open(file_path, 'rb') as f:
    95→            first_bytes = f.read(HTML_SNIFF_BYTES)
    96→            if first_bytes.startswith(b'<!DOCTYPE html') or first_bytes.startswith(b'<html'):
    97→                return False, "File appears to be an HTML error page rather than a LoRA file"
    98→    except OSError as e:
    99→        headless_logger.warning(f"Could not read first bytes of {filename} for HTML check: {e}")
   100→
   101→    return True, f"File validated successfully ({file_size:,} bytes)"
   102→
   103→def check_loras_in_directory(lora_dir: Path | str, fix_issues: bool = False) -> dict:
   104→    """
   105→    Checks all LoRA files in a directory for integrity issues.
   106→
   107→    Args:
   108→        lora_dir: Directory containing LoRA files
   109→        fix_issues: If True, removes corrupted files
   110→
   111→    Returns:
   112→        Dictionary with validation results
   113→    """
   114→    lora_dir = Path(lora_dir)
   115→    if not lora_dir.exists():
   116→        return {"error": f"Directory does not exist: {lora_dir}"}
   117→
   118→    results = {
   119→        "total_files": 0,
   120→        "valid_files": 0,
   121→        "invalid_files": 0,
   122→        "issues": [],
   123→        "summary": []
   124→    }
   125→
   126→    # Look for LoRA-like files
   127→    lora_extensions = ['.safetensors', '.bin', '.pt', '.pth']
   128→    lora_files = []
   129→
   130→    for ext in lora_extensions:
   131→        lora_files.extend(lora_dir.glob(f"*{ext}"))
   132→        lora_files.extend(lora_dir.glob(f"**/*{ext}"))  # Include subdirectories
   133→
   134→    # Filter to likely LoRA files
   135→    lora_files = [f for f in lora_files if 'lora' in f.name.lower() or f.suffix == '.safetensors']
   136→
   137→    results["total_files"] = len(lora_files)
   138→
   139→    for lora_file in lora_files:
   140→        is_valid, validation_msg = validate_lora_file(lora_file, lora_file.name)
   141→
   142→        if is_valid:
   143→            results["valid_files"] += 1
   144→            results["summary"].append(f"OK {lora_file.name}: {validation_msg}")
   145→        else:
   146→            results["invalid_files"] += 1
   147→            issue_msg = f"FAIL {lora_file.name}: {validation_msg}"
   148→            results["issues"].append(issue_msg)
   149→            results["summary"].append(issue_msg)
   150→
   151→            if fix_issues:
   152→                try:
   153→                    lora_file.unlink()
   154→                    results["summary"].append(f"  -> Removed corrupted file: {lora_file.name}")
   155→                except OSError as e:
   156→                    results["summary"].append(f"  -> Failed to remove {lora_file.name}: {e}")
   157→
   158→    return results
   159→
   160→def _normalize_activated_loras_list(current_activated) -> list:
   161→    """Helper to ensure activated_loras is a proper list."""
   162→    if not isinstance(current_activated, list):
   163→        try:
   164→            return [str(item).strip() for item in str(current_activated).split(',') if item.strip()]
   165→        except (ValueError, TypeError) as e:
   166→            headless_logger.debug(f"[WARNING] Failed to normalize activated_loras value '{current_activated}': {e}")
   167→            return []
   168→    return current_activated
   169→
   170→def _apply_special_lora_settings(task_id: str, lora_type: str, lora_basename: str, default_steps: int,
   171→                                 guidance_scale: float, flow_shift: float, ui_defaults: dict,
   172→                                 task_params_dict: dict, tea_cache_setting: float = None):
   173→    """
   174→    Shared helper to apply special LoRA settings (CausVid, LightI2X, etc.) to ui_defaults.
   175→    """
   176→    headless_logger.essential(f"Applying {lora_type} LoRA settings.", task_id=task_id)
   177→
   178→    # [STEPS DEBUG] Add detailed debug for steps logic
   179→    headless_logger.debug(f"[STEPS DEBUG] {lora_type}: task_params_dict keys: {list(task_params_dict.keys())}")
   180→    if "steps" in task_params_dict:
   181→        headless_logger.debug(f"[STEPS DEBUG] {lora_type}: Found 'steps' = {task_params_dict['steps']}")
   182→    if "num_inference_steps" in task_params_dict:
   183→        headless_logger.debug(f"[STEPS DEBUG] {lora_type}: Found 'num_inference_steps' = {task_params_dict['num_inference_steps']}")
   184→    if "video_length" in task_params_dict:
   185→        headless_logger.debug(f"[STEPS DEBUG] {lora_type}: Found 'video_length' = {task_params_dict['video_length']}")
   186→
   187→    # Handle steps logic
   188→    if "steps" in task_params_dict:
   189→        ui_defaults["num_inference_steps"] = task_params_dict["steps"]
   190→        headless_logger.essential(f"{lora_type} task using specified steps: {ui_defaults['num_inference_steps']}", task_id=task_id)
   191→    elif "num_inference_steps" in task_params_dict:
   192→        ui_defaults["num_inference_steps"] = task_params_dict["num_inference_steps"]
   193→        headless_logger.essential(f"{lora_type} task using specified num_inference_steps: {ui_defaults['num_inference_steps']}", task_id=task_id)
   194→    else:
   195→        ui_defaults["num_inference_steps"] = default_steps
   196→        headless_logger.essential(f"{lora_type} task defaulting to steps: {ui_defaults['num_inference_steps']}", task_id=task_id)
   197→
   198→    # Set guidance and flow shift
   199→    ui_defaults["guidance_scale"] = guidance_scale
   200→    ui_defaults["flow_shift"] = flow_shift
   201→
   202→    # Set tea cache if specified
   203→    if tea_cache_setting is not None:
   204→        ui_defaults["tea_cache_setting"] = tea_cache_setting
   205→
   206→    # Handle LoRA activation
   207→    current_activated = _normalize_activated_loras_list(ui_defaults.get("activated_loras", []))
   208→
   209→    if lora_basename not in current_activated:
   210→        current_activated.append(lora_basename)
   211→    ui_defaults["activated_loras"] = current_activated
   212→
   213→    # Handle multipliers - simple approach for build_task_state
   214→    current_multipliers_str = ui_defaults.get("loras_multipliers", "")
   215→    multipliers_list = [m.strip() for m in current_multipliers_str.split(" ") if m.strip()] if current_multipliers_str else []
   216→    while len(multipliers_list) < len(current_activated):
   217→        multipliers_list.insert(0, "1.0")
   218→    ui_defaults["loras_multipliers"] = " ".join(multipliers_list)
   219→
```

```
    21→def _get_task_type_directory(task_type: str) -> str:
    22→    """
    23→    Map task types to their output subdirectories.
    24→
    25→    Single-level directory structure: outputs/{task_type}/{files}
    26→    Examples:
    27→    - vace -> outputs/vace/
    28→    - travel_orchestrator -> outputs/travel_orchestrator/
    29→    - extract_frame -> outputs/extract_frame/

    33→
    34→    Returns:
    35→        Task type as directory name (e.g., 'vace', 'travel_orchestrator')
    36→    """

    41→def prepare_output_path(
    42→    task_id: str,

    53→    the output is placed in a subdirectory based on task_type
    54→    or directly in `main_output_dir_base` (backwards compatibility).
    55→
    56→    Args:
    57→        task_id: Unique task identifier
    58→        filename: Output filename
    59→        main_output_dir_base: Base output directory (from worker --main-output-dir)
    60→        task_type: Optional task type for subdirectory organization
    61→        custom_output_dir: Optional custom output directory (overrides all)

    63→    Returns:
    64→        Tuple of (Path object for file location, string path for database)
    65→    """
    66→    # Decide base directory for the file

    68→        output_dir_for_task = Path(custom_output_dir)
    69→        headless_logger.debug(f"Task {task_id}: Using custom output directory: {output_dir_for_task}", task_id=task_id)
    70→    else:
    71→        # Create task-type-specific subdirectory structure

    73→            type_subdir = _get_task_type_directory(task_type)

    85→                f"(backwards compatibility)",

    87→            )
    88→

    93→        uuid_pattern = r'_\d{6}_[a-f0-9]{6}\.(mp4|png|jpg|jpeg)$'
    94→        has_uuid_pattern = re.search(uuid_pattern, filename, re.IGNORECASE)
    95→

    97→            filename = f"{task_id}_{filename}"
    98→

   100→

   102→

   104→    if final_save_path.exists():
   105→        stem = final_save_path.stem

   117→    except ValueError:
```
