```
   121→
   122→    return final_save_path, db_output_location
   123→
   124→def sanitize_filename_for_storage(filename: str) -> str:
   125→    """
   126→    Sanitizes a filename to be safe for storage systems like Supabase Storage.
   127→
   128→    Removes characters that are invalid for S3/Supabase storage keys:
   129→    - Control characters (0x00-0x1F, 0x7F-0x9F)
   130→    - Special characters: etc.
   131→    - Path separators and other problematic characters
   132→
   133→    Args:
   134→        filename: Original filename that may contain unsafe characters
   135→
   136→    Returns:
   137→        Sanitized filename safe for storage systems
   138→    """
   139→    import re
   140→
   141→    # Define characters to remove (includes the special character causing the issue)
   142→    # This is based on S3/Supabase storage key restrictions and common filesystem issues
   143→    unsafe_chars = r'[\u00a7\u00ae\u00a9\u2122@\u00b7\u00ba\u00bd\u00be\u00bf\u00a1~\x00-\x1F\x7F-\x9F<>:"/\\|?*,]'
   144→
   145→    # Replace unsafe characters with empty string
   146→    sanitized = re.sub(unsafe_chars, '', filename)
   147→
   148→    # Replace multiple consecutive spaces/dots with single ones
   149→    sanitized = re.sub(r'[ \.]{2,}', ' ', sanitized)
   150→
   151→    # Remove leading/trailing whitespace and dots
   152→    sanitized = sanitized.strip(' .')
   153→
   154→    # Ensure we have a non-empty filename
   155→    if not sanitized:
   156→        sanitized = "sanitized_file"
   157→
   158→    return sanitized
   159→
   160→def prepare_output_path_with_upload(
   161→    task_id: str,
   162→    filename: str,
   163→    main_output_dir_base: Path,
   164→    task_type: str | None = None,  # NEW PARAMETER
   165→    *,
   166→    custom_output_dir: str | Path | None = None
   167→) -> tuple[Path, str]:
   168→    """
   169→    Prepares the output path for a task's artifact and handles Supabase upload if configured.
   170→
   171→    Args:
   172→        task_id: Unique task identifier
   173→        filename: Output filename
   174→        main_output_dir_base: Base output directory (from worker --main-output-dir)
   175→        task_type: Optional task type for subdirectory organization
   176→        custom_output_dir: Optional custom output directory (overrides all)
   177→
   178→    Returns:
   179→        tuple[Path, str]: (local_file_path, db_output_location)
   180→        - local_file_path: Where to save the file locally (for generation)
   181→        - db_output_location: What to store in the database (local path or Supabase URL)
   182→    """
   183→    # Sanitize filename BEFORE any processing to prevent storage upload issues
   184→    original_filename = filename
   185→    sanitized_filename = sanitize_filename_for_storage(filename)
   186→
   187→    if original_filename != sanitized_filename:
   188→        headless_logger.debug(f"Task {task_id}: Sanitized filename '{original_filename}' -> '{sanitized_filename}'", task_id=task_id)
   189→
   190→    # First, get the local path where we'll save the file (using sanitized filename)
   191→    # Forward task_type parameter
   192→    local_save_path, initial_db_location = prepare_output_path(
   193→        task_id, sanitized_filename, main_output_dir_base,
   194→        task_type=task_type,  # Forward task_type
   195→        custom_output_dir=custom_output_dir
   196→    )
   197→
   198→    # Return the local path for now - we'll handle Supabase upload after file is created
   199→    return local_save_path, initial_db_location
   200→
   201→def upload_and_get_final_output_location(
   202→    local_file_path: Path,
   203→    supabase_object_name: str,  # This parameter is now unused but kept for compatibility
   204→    initial_db_location: str) -> str:
   205→    """
   206→    Returns the local file path. Upload is now handled by the edge function.
   207→
   208→    Args:
   209→        local_file_path: Path to the local file
   210→        supabase_object_name: Unused (kept for compatibility)
   211→        initial_db_location: The initial DB location (local path)
   212→
   213→    Returns:
   214→        str: Local file path (upload now handled by edge function)
   215→    """
   216→    # Edge function will handle the upload, so we just return the local path
   217→    headless_logger.debug(f"File ready for edge function upload: {local_file_path}")
   218→    return str(local_file_path.resolve())
   219→
   220→def upload_intermediate_file_to_storage(
   221→    local_file_path: Path,
   222→    task_id: str,
   223→    filename: str) -> str | None:
   224→    """
   225→    Upload an intermediate file to Supabase storage for cross-worker access.
   226→
   227→    This is used when orchestrators create intermediate files (like reversed videos)
   228→    that need to be accessible by child tasks running on different workers.
   229→
   230→    Includes retry logic for transient failures (502/503/504, timeouts, network errors).
   231→
   232→    Args:
   233→        local_file_path: Path to the local file to upload
   234→        task_id: Task ID for organizing uploads
   235→        filename: Filename to use in storage
   236→
   237→    Returns:
   238→        Public URL of the uploaded file, or None on failure
   239→    """
   240→    import httpx
   241→    import mimetypes
   242→
   243→    # Retry configuration
   244→    RETRYABLE_STATUS_CODES = {502, 503, 504}
   245→    MAX_RETRIES = 3
   246→
   247→    # Check if Supabase is configured (read URL from canonical config set by worker.py)
   248→    from source.core.db import config as _db_config
   249→    SUPABASE_URL = _db_config.SUPABASE_URL
   250→    SUPABASE_KEY = os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") or os.environ.get("SUPABASE_ANON_KEY")
   251→
   252→    if not SUPABASE_URL or not SUPABASE_KEY:
   253→        headless_logger.warning(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] Supabase not configured, cannot upload", task_id=task_id)
   254→        return None
   255→
   256→    if not local_file_path.exists():
   257→        headless_logger.warning(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] File not found: {local_file_path}", task_id=task_id)
   258→        return None
   259→
   260→    try:
   261→        headers = {
   262→            "Authorization": f"Bearer {SUPABASE_KEY}",
   263→            "apikey": SUPABASE_KEY,
   264→            "Content-Type": "application/json"
   265→        }
   266→
   267→        # Step 1: Get signed upload URL - WITH RETRY
   268→        generate_url_edge = f"{SUPABASE_URL.rstrip('/')}/functions/v1/generate-upload-url"
   269→        content_type = mimetypes.guess_type(str(local_file_path))[0] or 'application/octet-stream'
   270→
   271→        upload_url_resp = None
   272→        for attempt in range(MAX_RETRIES):
   273→            try:
   274→                upload_url_resp = httpx.post(
   275→                    generate_url_edge,
   276→                    headers=headers,
   277→                    json={
   278→                        "task_id": task_id,
   279→                        "filename": filename,
   280→                        "content_type": content_type
   281→                    },
   282→                    timeout=30 + (attempt * 15)
   283→                )
   284→
   285→                if upload_url_resp.status_code == 200:
   286→                    break
   287→                elif upload_url_resp.status_code in RETRYABLE_STATUS_CODES and attempt < MAX_RETRIES - 1:
   288→                    wait_time = 2 ** attempt
   289→                    headless_logger.warning(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] generate-upload-url got {upload_url_resp.status_code}, retrying in {wait_time}s...", task_id=task_id)
   290→                    time.sleep(wait_time)
   291→                    continue
   292→                else:
   293→                    break  # Non-retryable error
   294→
   295→            except (httpx.HTTPError, OSError, ValueError) as e:
   296→                if attempt < MAX_RETRIES - 1:
   297→                    wait_time = 2 ** attempt
   298→                    headless_logger.warning(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] generate-upload-url error, retrying in {wait_time}s: {e}", task_id=task_id)
   299→                    time.sleep(wait_time)
   300→                    continue
   301→                else:
   302→                    headless_logger.error(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] [EDGE_FAIL:generate-upload-url:NETWORK] {e}", task_id=task_id)
   303→                    return None
   304→
   305→        if not upload_url_resp or upload_url_resp.status_code != 200:
   306→            error_text = upload_url_resp.text[:200] if upload_url_resp else "No response"
   307→            error_code = upload_url_resp.status_code if upload_url_resp else "N/A"
   308→            headless_logger.error(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] [EDGE_FAIL:generate-upload-url:HTTP_{error_code}] {error_text}", task_id=task_id)
   309→            return None
   310→
   311→        upload_data = upload_url_resp.json()
   312→        upload_url = upload_data.get("upload_url")
   313→        storage_path = upload_data.get("storage_path")
   314→
   315→        if not upload_url:
   316→            headless_logger.error(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] No upload_url in response", task_id=task_id)
   317→            return None
   318→
   319→        # Step 2: Upload file via signed URL - WITH RETRY
   320→        # IMPORTANT: do NOT read entire file into memory (can be large).
   321→        file_size_mb = local_file_path.stat().st_size / BYTES_PER_KIBIBYTE / BYTES_PER_KIBIBYTE
   322→        headless_logger.debug(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] Uploading {local_file_path.name} ({file_size_mb:.1f} MB)", task_id=task_id)
   323→
   324→        put_resp = None
   325→        for attempt in range(MAX_RETRIES):
   326→            try:
   327→                with open(local_file_path, 'rb') as f:
   328→                    put_resp = httpx.put(
   329→                        upload_url,
   330→                        headers={"Content-Type": content_type},
   331→                        content=f,
   332→                        timeout=300 + (attempt * 60)  # 5 min base, +1 min per retry
   333→                    )
   334→
   335→                if put_resp.status_code in [200, 201]:
   336→                    break
   337→                elif put_resp.status_code in RETRYABLE_STATUS_CODES and attempt < MAX_RETRIES - 1:
   338→                    wait_time = 2 ** attempt
   339→                    headless_logger.warning(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] storage-upload got {put_resp.status_code}, retrying in {wait_time}s...", task_id=task_id)
   340→                    time.sleep(wait_time)
   341→                    continue
   342→                else:
   343→                    break  # Non-retryable error
   344→
   345→            except (httpx.HTTPError, OSError, ValueError) as e:
   346→                if attempt < MAX_RETRIES - 1:
   347→                    wait_time = 2 ** attempt
   348→                    headless_logger.warning(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] storage-upload error, retrying in {wait_time}s: {e}", task_id=task_id)
   349→                    time.sleep(wait_time)
   350→                    continue
   351→                else:
   352→                    headless_logger.error(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] [EDGE_FAIL:storage-upload:NETWORK] {e}", task_id=task_id)
   353→                    return None
   354→
   355→        if not put_resp or put_resp.status_code not in [200, 201]:
   356→            error_text = put_resp.text[:200] if put_resp else "No response"
   357→            error_code = put_resp.status_code if put_resp else "N/A"
   358→            headless_logger.error(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] [EDGE_FAIL:storage-upload:HTTP_{error_code}] {error_text}", task_id=task_id)
   359→            return None
   360→
   361→        # Construct public URL
   362→        public_url = f"{SUPABASE_URL}/storage/v1/object/public/image_uploads/{storage_path}"
   363→        headless_logger.debug(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] Uploaded to: {public_url}", task_id=task_id)
   364→
   365→        return public_url
   366→
   367→    except (httpx.HTTPError, OSError, ValueError) as e:
   368→        headless_logger.error(f"[UPLOAD_INTERMEDIATE] Exception: {e}", task_id=task_id)
   369→        traceback.print_exc()
   370→        return None
   371→
   372→def wait_for_file_stable(path: Path | str, checks: int = 3, interval: float = 1.0) -> bool:
   373→    """Return True when the file size stays constant for a few consecutive checks.
   374→    Useful to make sure long-running encoders have finished writing before we
   375→    copy/move the file.
   376→    """
   377→    p = Path(path)
   378→    if not p.exists():
   379→        return False
   380→    last_size = p.stat().st_size
   381→    stable_count = 0
   382→    for _ in range(checks):
   383→        time.sleep(interval)
   384→        new_size = p.stat().st_size
   385→        if new_size == last_size and new_size > 0:
   386→            stable_count += 1
   387→            if stable_count >= checks - 1:
   388→                return True
   389→        else:
   390→            stable_count = 0
   391→            last_size = new_size
   392→    return False
   393→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Core generation parameters.
     3→"""
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional
     7→
     8→from .base import ParamGroup
     9→
    10→
    11→@dataclass
    12→class GenerationConfig(ParamGroup):
    13→    """
    14→    Core generation parameters.
    15→    
    16→    These are the fundamental parameters for video generation
    17→    that don't fit into specialized categories (LoRA, VACE, Phase).
    18→    """
    19→    prompt: str = ""
    20→    negative_prompt: Optional[str] = None
    21→    resolution: Optional[str] = None
    22→    video_length: Optional[int] = None
    23→    num_inference_steps: Optional[int] = None
    24→    guidance_scale: Optional[float] = None
    25→    seed: Optional[int] = None
    26→    flow_shift: Optional[float] = None
    27→    sample_solver: Optional[str] = None
    28→    
    29→    # Image inputs
    30→    image_start: Optional[str] = None
    31→    image_end: Optional[str] = None
    32→    image_refs: Optional[List[str]] = None
    33→    
    34→    # Advanced parameters
    35→    embedded_guidance_scale: Optional[float] = None
    36→    denoising_strength: Optional[float] = None
    37→    
    38→    @classmethod
    39→    def from_params(cls, params: Dict[str, Any], **context) -> 'GenerationConfig':
    40→        """Parse generation config from parameters."""
    41→        # Handle image parameter aliases for img2img tasks
    42→        # If 'image' or 'image_url' is provided but not 'image_start', use it as image_start
    43→        image_start = params.get('image_start')
    44→        if not image_start:
    45→            image_start = params.get('image') or params.get('image_url')
    46→
    47→        return cls(
    48→            prompt=params.get('prompt', ''),
    49→            negative_prompt=params.get('negative_prompt'),
    50→            resolution=params.get('resolution'),
    51→            video_length=params.get('video_length'),
    52→            num_inference_steps=params.get('num_inference_steps') or params.get('steps'),
    53→            guidance_scale=params.get('guidance_scale'),
    54→            seed=params.get('seed'),
    55→            flow_shift=params.get('flow_shift'),
    56→            sample_solver=params.get('sample_solver'),
    57→            image_start=image_start,
    58→            image_end=params.get('image_end'),
    59→            image_refs=params.get('image_refs'),
    60→            embedded_guidance_scale=params.get('embedded_guidance_scale'),
    61→            denoising_strength=params.get('denoising_strength') or params.get('denoise_strength') or params.get('strength'),
    62→        )
    63→    
    64→    def to_wgp_format(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    65→        """Convert to WGP-compatible format."""
    66→        result = {}
    67→        
    68→        if self.prompt:
    69→            result['prompt'] = self.prompt
    70→        if self.negative_prompt:
    71→            result['negative_prompt'] = self.negative_prompt
    72→        if self.resolution:
    73→            result['resolution'] = self.resolution
    74→        if self.video_length is not None:
    75→            result['video_length'] = self.video_length
    76→        if self.num_inference_steps is not None:
    77→            result['num_inference_steps'] = self.num_inference_steps
    78→        if self.guidance_scale is not None:
    79→            result['guidance_scale'] = self.guidance_scale
    80→        if self.seed is not None:
    81→            result['seed'] = self.seed
    82→        if self.flow_shift is not None:
    83→            result['flow_shift'] = self.flow_shift
    84→        if self.sample_solver:
    85→            result['sample_solver'] = self.sample_solver
    86→        if self.image_start:
    87→            result['image_start'] = self.image_start
    88→        if self.image_end:
    89→            result['image_end'] = self.image_end
    90→        if self.image_refs:
    91→            result['image_refs'] = self.image_refs
    92→        if self.embedded_guidance_scale is not None:
    93→            result['embedded_guidance_scale'] = self.embedded_guidance_scale
    94→        if self.denoising_strength is not None:
    95→            result['denoising_strength'] = self.denoising_strength
    96→        
    97→        return result
    98→    
    99→    def validate(self) -> list:
   100→        """Validate generation configuration."""
   101→        errors = []
   102→        
   103→        if self.video_length is not None:
   104→            if (self.video_length - 1) % 4 != 0:
   105→                errors.append(f"video_length {self.video_length} is not 4N+1")
   106→        
   107→        return errors
```

```
1→"""
     2→TaskConfig - the main typed configuration object.
     3→
     4→This combines all parameter groups and handles:
     5→- Parsing from DB tasks
     6→- Parsing from segment parameters
     7→- Converting to WGP format
     8→"""
     9→
    10→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    11→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Callable
    12→import logging
    13→
    14→from .base import ParamGroup
    15→from .lora import LoRAConfig
    16→from .vace import VACEConfig
    17→from .generation import GenerationConfig
    18→from .phase import PhaseConfig
    19→
    20→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    21→
    22→
    23→@dataclass
    24→class TaskConfig(ParamGroup):
    25→    """
    26→    Complete task configuration combining all parameter groups.
    27→    
    28→    This is the main entry point for typed parameter handling.
    29→    """
    30→    generation: GenerationConfig = field(default_factory=GenerationConfig)
    31→    lora: LoRAConfig = field(default_factory=LoRAConfig)
    32→    vace: VACEConfig = field(default_factory=VACEConfig)
    33→    phase: PhaseConfig = field(default_factory=PhaseConfig)
    34→    
    35→    # Task metadata
    36→    task_id: str = ""
    37→    task_type: str = ""
    38→    model: str = ""
    39→    
    40→    # Extra params that don't fit elsewhere (passthrough)
    41→    extra_params: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    42→    
    43→    @classmethod
    44→    def from_params(cls, params: Dict[str, Any], **context) -> 'TaskConfig':
    45→        """
    46→        Parse TaskConfig from a flat parameter dict.
    47→        
    48→        Use from_db_task() for DB tasks that need flattening first.
    49→        """
    50→        task_id = context.get('task_id', '')
    51→        task_type = context.get('task_type', '')
    52→        model = context.get('model', '') or params.get('model_name', '')
    53→        
    54→        # Parse each component
    55→        generation = GenerationConfig.from_params(params, **context)
    56→        vace = VACEConfig.from_params(params, **context)
    57→        phase = PhaseConfig.from_params(params, **context)
    58→        
    59→        # Parse LoRA config
    60→        lora = LoRAConfig.from_params(params, **context)
    61→        
    62→        # If phase config has LoRAs, merge them in
    63→        if not phase.is_empty():
    64→            lora_info = phase.get_lora_info()
    65→            if lora_info.get('additional_loras'):
    66→                phase_lora = LoRAConfig.from_params({
    67→                    'additional_loras': lora_info['additional_loras'],
    68→                    'lora_names': lora_info.get('lora_names', []),
    69→                    'lora_multipliers': lora_info.get('lora_multipliers', []),
    70→                }, **context)
    71→                lora = lora.merge(phase_lora)
    72→        
    73→        # Collect extra params (everything not handled by specific groups)
    74→        handled_keys = {
    75→            'prompt', 'negative_prompt', 'resolution', 'video_length',
    76→            'num_inference_steps', 'steps', 'guidance_scale', 'seed',
    77→            'flow_shift', 'sample_solver', 'image_start', 'image_end', 'image_refs',
    78→            'embedded_guidance_scale', 'denoising_strength', 'denoise_strength',
    79→            'video_guide', 'video_mask', 'video_prompt_type',
    80→            'control_net_weight', 'control_net_weight2',
    81→            'activated_loras', 'lora_names', 'loras_multipliers', 'lora_multipliers',
    82→            'additional_loras', 'phase_config', 'model_name',
    83→            # Image-to-image parameter aliases
    84→            'image', 'image_url', 'strength',
    85→        }
    86→        extra_params = {k: v for k, v in params.items() if k not in handled_keys}
    87→        
    88→        return cls(
    89→            generation=generation,
    90→            lora=lora,
    91→            vace=vace,
    92→            phase=phase,
    93→            task_id=task_id,
    94→            task_type=task_type,
    95→            model=model,
    96→            extra_params=extra_params,
    97→        )
    98→    
    99→    @classmethod
   100→    def from_db_task(cls, db_params: Dict[str, Any], **context) -> 'TaskConfig':
   101→        """
   102→        Parse TaskConfig from DB task parameters.
   103→        
   104→        Handles nested orchestrator_payload/orchestrator_details with proper precedence.
   105→        """
   106→        # Flatten with precedence
   107→        flat_params = ParamGroup.flatten_params(db_params, context.get('task_id', ''))
   108→        return cls.from_params(flat_params, **context)
   109→    
   110→    @classmethod
   111→    def from_segment_params(
   112→        cls,
   113→        segment_params: Dict[str, Any],
   114→        orchestrator_payload: Dict[str, Any],
   115→        individual_params: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
   116→        **context
   117→    ) -> 'TaskConfig':
   118→        """
   119→        Parse TaskConfig from segment parameters with proper precedence.
   120→        
   121→        Precedence: individual_params > segment_params > orchestrator_payload
   122→        """
   123→        # Build merged params with precedence
   124→        merged = {}
   125→        
   126→        # Start with orchestrator_payload (lowest)
   127→        if orchestrator_payload:
   128→            merged.update(orchestrator_payload)
   129→        
   130→        # Then segment_params (medium)
   131→        if segment_params:
   132→            merged.update(segment_params)
   133→        
   134→        # Finally individual_params (highest)
   135→        if individual_params:
   136→            merged.update(individual_params)
   137→        
   138→        return cls.from_params(merged, **context)
   139→    
   140→    def to_wgp_format(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   141→        """
   142→        Convert to WGP-compatible parameter dict.
   143→        
   144→        This is the single point of conversion to WGP format.
   145→        """
   146→        result = {}
   147→        
   148→        # Add generation params
   149→        result.update(self.generation.to_wgp_format())
   150→        
   151→        # Add LoRA params
   152→        result.update(self.lora.to_wgp_format())
   153→        
   154→        # CRITICAL: Also include pending downloads so downstream can handle them
   155→        if self.lora.has_pending_downloads():
   156→            result['additional_loras'] = self.lora.get_pending_downloads()
   157→        
   158→        # Add VACE params
   159→        result.update(self.vace.to_wgp_format())
   160→        
   161→        # Add phase config params
   162→        if not self.phase.is_empty():
   163→            phase_params = self.phase.to_wgp_format()
   164→            
   165→            # Don't duplicate LoRA params from phase (already handled above)
   166→            for key in ['lora_names', 'lora_multipliers', 'additional_loras']:
   167→                phase_params.pop(key, None)
   168→            
   169→            result.update(phase_params)
   170→            
   171→            # If phase config requires patching, pass the info through
   172→            if self.phase.has_patch_config():
   173→                result['_parsed_phase_config'] = self.phase.parsed_output
   174→                result['_phase_config_model_name'] = self.phase.patch_model_name or self.model
   175→        
   176→        # Add model
   177→        if self.model:
   178→            result['model'] = self.model
   179→        
   180→        # Add extra params (passthrough)
   181→        result.update(self.extra_params)
   182→        
   183→        return result
   184→    
   185→    def validate(self) -> List[str]:
   186→        """Validate entire task configuration."""
   187→        errors = []
   188→        
   189→        errors.extend(self.generation.validate())
   190→        errors.extend(self.lora.validate())
   191→        errors.extend(self.vace.validate())
   192→        errors.extend(self.phase.validate())
   193→        
   194→        # Check LoRA/multiplier count match
   195→        lora_format = self.lora.to_wgp_format()
   196→        lora_count = len(lora_format.get('activated_loras', []))
   197→        mult_str = lora_format.get('loras_multipliers', '')
   198→        
   199→        if lora_count > 0 and mult_str:
   200→            mult_str_stripped = mult_str.strip()
   201→            if mult_str_stripped:
   202→                # Count multipliers based on format
   203→                if ';' in mult_str_stripped:
   204→                    mult_count = len(mult_str_stripped.split())
   205→                elif ',' in mult_str_stripped:
   206→                    mult_count = len(mult_str_stripped.split(','))
   207→                else:
   208→                    mult_count = len(mult_str_stripped.split())
   209→                
   210→                if mult_count != lora_count:
   211→                    errors.append(f"LoRA count ({lora_count}) != multiplier count ({mult_count})")
   212→        
   213→        return errors
   214→    
   215→    def log_summary(self, log_func: Callable = None):
   216→        """Log a summary of the configuration."""
   217→        if log_func is None:
   218→            log_func = logger.info
   219→        
   220→        log_func(f"[TaskConfig] Task {self.task_id} ({self.task_type})")
   221→        log_func(f"  Model: {self.model}")
   222→        log_func(f"  Resolution: {self.generation.resolution}")
   223→        log_func(f"  Video length: {self.generation.video_length}")
   224→        log_func(f"  Steps: {self.generation.num_inference_steps}")
   225→        log_func(f"  LoRAs: {len(self.lora.entries)} entries, {len([e for e in self.lora.entries if e.status.value == 'pending'])} pending")
   226→        if not self.phase.is_empty():
   227→            log_func(f"  Phase config: {self.phase.num_phases} phases, {self.phase.total_steps} steps")
   228→
```

```
1→from source.core.log import headless_logger
     2→
     3→# Re-export from canonical location for backward compatibility
     4→from source.core.params.phase_config_parser import parse_phase_config, DIFFUSION_TIMESTEP_SCALE
     5→
     6→# Target megapixel count for auto-scaling img2img input images
     7→IMG2IMG_TARGET_MEGAPIXELS = 1024 * 1024
     8→
     9→# Default resolution string for image generation tasks
    10→DEFAULT_IMAGE_RESOLUTION = "1024x1024"
    11→from source.models.model_handlers.qwen_handler import QwenHandler
    12→from source.utils import extract_orchestrator_parameters
    13→from headless_model_management import GenerationTask
    14→
    15→def db_task_to_generation_task(db_task_params: dict, task_id: str, task_type: str, wan2gp_path: str, debug_mode: bool = False) -> GenerationTask:
    16→    """
    17→    Convert a database task row to a GenerationTask object for the queue system.
    18→    """
    19→    headless_logger.debug(f"Converting DB task to GenerationTask", task_id=task_id)
    20→
    21→    prompt = db_task_params.get("prompt", "")
    22→
    23→    # For img2img tasks, empty prompt is acceptable (will use minimal changes)
    24→    # Provide a minimal default prompt to avoid errors
    25→    img2img_task_types = {"z_image_turbo_i2i", "qwen_image_edit", "qwen_image_style", "image_inpaint"}
    26→    if not prompt:
    27→        if task_type in img2img_task_types:
    28→            prompt = " "  # Minimal prompt for img2img
    29→            headless_logger.debug(f"Task {task_id}: Using minimal prompt for img2img task", task_id=task_id)
    30→        else:
    31→            raise ValueError(f"Task {task_id}: prompt is required")
    32→    
    33→    model = db_task_params.get("model")
    34→    if not model:
    35→        from source.task_handlers.tasks.task_types import get_default_model
    36→        model = get_default_model(task_type)
    37→    
    38→    generation_params = {}
    39→    
    40→    param_whitelist = {
    41→        "negative_prompt", "resolution", "video_length", "num_inference_steps",
    42→        "guidance_scale", "seed", "embedded_guidance_scale", "flow_shift",
    43→        "audio_guidance_scale", "repeat_generation", "multi_images_gen_type",
    44→        "guidance2_scale", "guidance3_scale", "guidance_phases", "switch_threshold", "switch_threshold2", "model_switch_phase",
    45→        "video_guide", "video_mask",
    46→        "video_prompt_type", "control_net_weight", "control_net_weight2",
    47→        "keep_frames_video_guide", "video_guide_outpainting", "mask_expand",
    48→        "image_prompt_type", "image_start", "image_end", "image_refs",
    49→        "frames_positions", "image_guide", "image_mask",
    50→        "model_mode", "video_source", "keep_frames_video_source",
    51→        "audio_guide", "audio_guide2", "audio_source", "audio_prompt_type", "speakers_locations",
    52→        "activated_loras", "loras_multipliers", "additional_loras", "loras",
    53→        "tea_cache_setting", "tea_cache_start_step_perc", "RIFLEx_setting",
    54→        "slg_switch", "slg_layers", "slg_start_perc", "slg_end_perc",
    55→        "cfg_star_switch", "cfg_zero_step", "prompt_enhancer",
    56→        # Hires fix parameters
    57→        "hires_scale", "hires_steps", "hires_denoise", "hires_upscale_method", "lightning_lora_strength",
    58→        "sliding_window_size", "sliding_window_overlap", "sliding_window_overlap_noise",
    59→        "sliding_window_discard_last_frames", "latent_noise_mask_strength",
    60→        "vid2vid_init_video", "vid2vid_init_strength",
    61→        "remove_background_images_ref", "temporal_upsampling", "spatial_upsampling",
    62→        "film_grain_intensity", "film_grain_saturation",
    63→        "image_refs_relative_size",
    64→        "output_dir", "custom_output_dir",
    65→        "override_profile",
    66→        "image", "image_url", "mask_url",
    67→        "style_reference_image", "subject_reference_image",
    68→        "style_reference_strength", "subject_strength",
    69→        "subject_description", "in_this_scene",
    70→        "output_format", "enable_base64_output", "enable_sync_mode",
    71→        # Uni3C motion guidance parameters
    72→        "use_uni3c", "uni3c_guide_video", "uni3c_strength",
    73→        "uni3c_start_percent", "uni3c_end_percent",
    74→        "uni3c_keep_on_gpu", "uni3c_frame_policy",
    75→        "uni3c_zero_empty_frames", "uni3c_blackout_last_frame",
    76→        # Image-to-image parameters
    77→        "denoising_strength",
    78→    }
    79→    
    80→    for param in param_whitelist:
    81→        if param in db_task_params:
    82→            generation_params[param] = db_task_params[param]
    83→    
    84→    # Layer 1 Uni3C logging - detect whitelist failures early
    85→    if "use_uni3c" in generation_params:
    86→        headless_logger.info(
    87→            f"[UNI3C] Task {task_id}: use_uni3c={generation_params.get('use_uni3c')}, "
    88→            f"guide_video={generation_params.get('uni3c_guide_video', 'NOT_SET')}, "
    89→            f"strength={generation_params.get('uni3c_strength', 'NOT_SET')}"
    90→        )
    91→    elif db_task_params.get("use_uni3c"):
    92→        # CRITICAL: Detect when whitelist is missing the param
    93→        headless_logger.warning(
    94→            f"[UNI3C] Task {task_id}: ⚠️ use_uni3c was in db_task_params but NOT in generation_params! "
    95→            f"Check param_whitelist in task_conversion.py"
    96→        )
    97→    
    98→    # Extract orchestrator parameters
    99→    extracted_params = extract_orchestrator_parameters(db_task_params, task_id)
   100→
   101→    if "phase_config" in extracted_params:
   102→        db_task_params["phase_config"] = extracted_params["phase_config"]
   103→
   104→    # Copy additional_loras from extracted params if not already in generation_params
   105→    if "additional_loras" in extracted_params and "additional_loras" not in generation_params:
   106→        generation_params["additional_loras"] = extracted_params["additional_loras"]
   107→    
   108→    if "steps" in db_task_params and "num_inference_steps" not in generation_params:
   109→        generation_params["num_inference_steps"] = db_task_params["steps"]
   110→    
   111→    # Note: LoRA resolution is handled centrally in HeadlessTaskQueue._convert_to_wgp_task()
   112→    # via TaskConfig/LoRAConfig which detects URLs and downloads them automatically
   113→
   114→    # Qwen Task Handlers
   115→    qwen_handler = QwenHandler(
   116→        wan_root=wan2gp_path,
   117→        task_id=task_id)
   118→
   119→    if task_type == "qwen_image_edit":
   120→        qwen_handler.handle_qwen_image_edit(db_task_params, generation_params)
   121→    elif task_type == "qwen_image_hires":
   122→        qwen_handler.handle_qwen_image_hires(db_task_params, generation_params)
   123→    elif task_type == "image_inpaint":
   124→        qwen_handler.handle_image_inpaint(db_task_params, generation_params)
   125→    elif task_type == "annotated_image_edit":
   126→        qwen_handler.handle_annotated_image_edit(db_task_params, generation_params)
   127→    elif task_type == "qwen_image_style":
   128→        qwen_handler.handle_qwen_image_style(db_task_params, generation_params)
   129→        prompt = generation_params.get("prompt", prompt)
   130→        model = "qwen_image_edit_20B"
   131→    elif task_type == "qwen_image":
   132→        qwen_handler.handle_qwen_image(db_task_params, generation_params)
   133→        model = "qwen_image_edit_20B"
   134→    elif task_type == "qwen_image_2512":
   135→        qwen_handler.handle_qwen_image_2512(db_task_params, generation_params)
   136→        model = "qwen_image_2512_20B"
   137→    elif task_type == "z_image_turbo":
   138→        # Z-Image turbo - fast text-to-image generation
   139→        generation_params.setdefault("video_prompt_type", "")  # No input image
   140→        generation_params.setdefault("video_length", 1)  # Single image output
   141→        generation_params.setdefault("guidance_scale", 0)  # Z-Image uses guidance_scale=0
   142→        generation_params.setdefault("num_inference_steps", int(db_task_params.get("num_inference_steps", 8)))
   143→
   144→        # Resolution handling
   145→        if "resolution" in db_task_params:
   146→            generation_params["resolution"] = db_task_params["resolution"]
   147→
   148→        # Override model to use Z-Image (user might pass "z-image" with hyphen)
   149→        model = "z_image"
   150→
   151→    elif task_type == "z_image_turbo_i2i":
   152→        # Z-Image turbo img2img - fast image-to-image generation
   153→        import tempfile
   154→        from PIL import Image
   155→
   156→        # Get image URL
   157→        image_url = db_task_params.get("image") or db_task_params.get("image_url")
   158→        if not image_url:
   159→            raise ValueError(f"Task {task_id}: 'image' or 'image_url' required for z_image_turbo_i2i")
   160→
   161→        # Download image to local file (required for WGP)
   162→        local_image_path = None
   163→        try:
   164→            import requests
   165→            import os
   166→            headless_logger.debug(f"Downloading image for img2img: {image_url}", task_id=task_id)
   167→            response = requests.get(image_url, timeout=30)
   168→            response.raise_for_status()
   169→
   170→            # Save to temp file and keep it for WGP to use
   171→            with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(suffix=".png", delete=False) as tmp_file:
   172→                tmp_file.write(response.content)
   173→                local_image_path = tmp_file.name
   174→
   175→            headless_logger.info(f"[Z_IMAGE_I2I] Downloaded image to: {local_image_path}", task_id=task_id)
   176→
   177→            # Handle resolution - auto-detect from image or use provided
   178→            image_size = db_task_params.get("image_size", "auto")
   179→            if image_size == "auto" or not db_task_params.get("resolution"):
   180→                with Image.open(local_image_path) as img:
   181→                    width, height = img.size
   182→
   183→                    # Scale to approximately 1 megapixel (1024x1024) while preserving aspect ratio
   184→                    target_pixels = IMG2IMG_TARGET_MEGAPIXELS
   185→                    current_pixels = width * height
   186→
   187→                    if current_pixels > 0:
   188→                        # Calculate scale factor to reach target pixels
   189→                        import math
   190→                        scale = math.sqrt(target_pixels / current_pixels)
   191→
   192→                        # Apply scale to both dimensions
   193→                        scaled_width = int(round(width * scale))
   194→                        scaled_height = int(round(height * scale))
   195→
   196→                        # Round to nearest multiple of 8 (Z-Image requirement)
   197→                        scaled_width = (scaled_width // 8) * 8
   198→                        scaled_height = (scaled_height // 8) * 8
   199→
   200→                        # Ensure minimum size
   201→                        scaled_width = max(8, scaled_width)
   202→                        scaled_height = max(8, scaled_height)
   203→
   204→                        generation_params["resolution"] = f"{scaled_width}x{scaled_height}"
   205→                        headless_logger.info(
   206→                            f"Scaled resolution: {width}x{height} ({current_pixels:,} px) → "
   207→                            f"{scaled_width}x{scaled_height} ({scaled_width*scaled_height:,} px, scale={scale:.3f})",
   208→                            task_id=task_id
   209→                        )
   210→                    else:
   211→                        generation_params["resolution"] = DEFAULT_IMAGE_RESOLUTION
   212→            elif "resolution" in db_task_params:
   213→                generation_params["resolution"] = db_task_params["resolution"]
   214→            else:
   215→                generation_params["resolution"] = DEFAULT_IMAGE_RESOLUTION
   216→
   217→        except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
   218→            # If download fails, clean up and raise error
   219→            if local_image_path:
   220→                import os
   221→                try:
   222→                    os.unlink(local_image_path)
   223→                except OSError as e_cleanup:
   224→                    headless_logger.debug(f"Failed to clean up temp image file after download error: {e_cleanup}", task_id=task_id)
   225→            raise ValueError(f"Task {task_id}: Failed to download image for img2img: {e}")
   226→
   227→        # CRITICAL: Pass local file path (not URL) to WGP
   228→        generation_params["image_start"] = local_image_path
   229→
   230→        # Set img2img parameters
   231→        generation_params.setdefault("video_prompt_type", "")  # Image input handled via image_start
   232→        generation_params.setdefault("video_length", 1)  # Single image output
   233→        generation_params.setdefault("guidance_scale", 0)  # Z-Image uses guidance_scale=0
   234→        generation_params.setdefault("num_inference_steps", int(db_task_params.get("num_inference_steps", 12)))
   235→
   236→        # CRITICAL: Add denoising_strength to generation_params so it gets passed through
   237→        actual_strength = db_task_params.get("denoising_strength") or db_task_params.get("denoise_strength") or db_task_params.get("strength", 0.7)
   238→        generation_params["denoising_strength"] = actual_strength
   239→
   240→        headless_logger.info(
   241→            f"[Z_IMAGE_I2I] Setup complete - local_image={local_image_path}, resolution={generation_params['resolution']}, "
   242→            f"strength={actual_strength}, steps={generation_params['num_inference_steps']}",
   243→            task_id=task_id
   244→        )
   245→
   246→        # Override model to use Z-Image img2img
   247→        model = "z_image_img2img"
   248→
   249→    # Defaults
   250→    essential_defaults = {
   251→        "seed": -1,
   252→        "negative_prompt": "",
   253→    }
   254→    for param, default_value in essential_defaults.items():
   255→        if param not in generation_params:
   256→            generation_params[param] = default_value
   257→    
   258→    # Phase Config Override
   259→    if "phase_config" in db_task_params:
   260→        try:
   261→            steps_per_phase = db_task_params["phase_config"].get("steps_per_phase", [2, 2, 2])
   262→            phase_config_steps = sum(steps_per_phase)
   263→
   264→            parsed_phase_config = parse_phase_config(
   265→                phase_config=db_task_params["phase_config"],
   266→                num_inference_steps=phase_config_steps,
   267→                task_id=task_id,
   268→                model_name=model,
   269→                debug_mode=debug_mode
   270→            )
   271→
   272→            generation_params["num_inference_steps"] = phase_config_steps
   273→
   274→            for key in ["guidance_phases", "switch_threshold", "switch_threshold2",
   275→                       "guidance_scale", "guidance2_scale", "guidance3_scale",
   276→                       "flow_shift", "sample_solver", "model_switch_phase",
   277→                       "lora_names", "lora_multipliers", "additional_loras"]:
   278→                if key in parsed_phase_config and parsed_phase_config[key] is not None:
   279→                    generation_params[key] = parsed_phase_config[key]
   280→
   281→            if "lora_names" in parsed_phase_config:
   282→                generation_params["activated_loras"] = parsed_phase_config["lora_names"]
   283→            if "lora_multipliers" in parsed_phase_config:
   284→                generation_params["loras_multipliers"] = " ".join(str(m) for m in parsed_phase_config["lora_multipliers"])
   285→
   286→            if "_patch_config" in parsed_phase_config:
   287→                generation_params["_parsed_phase_config"] = parsed_phase_config
   288→                generation_params["_phase_config_model_name"] = model
   289→            
   290→            # Note: LoRA URL resolution is handled centrally in HeadlessTaskQueue._convert_to_wgp_task()
   291→            # URLs in activated_loras are detected by LoRAConfig.from_params() and downloaded there
   292→        except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as e:
   293→            raise ValueError(f"Task {task_id}: Invalid phase_config: {e}")
   294→
   295→    priority = db_task_params.get("priority", 0)
   296→    if task_type.endswith("_orchestrator"):
   297→        priority = max(priority, 10)
   298→    
   299→    generation_task = GenerationTask(
   300→        id=task_id,
   301→        model=model,
   302→        prompt=prompt,
   303→        parameters=generation_params,
   304→        priority=priority
   305→    )
   306→    
   307→    return generation_task
   308→
   309→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Qwen image compositing utilities.
     3→
     4→Handles resolution capping and mask-based image compositing
     5→for Qwen inpainting/annotation workflows.
     6→
     7→Extracted from qwen_handler.py.
     8→"""
     9→
    10→from io import BytesIO
    11→from pathlib import Path
    12→from typing import Optional
    13→
    14→import requests
    15→from PIL import Image  # type: ignore
    16→
    17→from source.core.log import model_logger
    18→
    19→# Maximum pixel dimension for Qwen image editing tasks
    20→QWEN_MAX_DIMENSION = 1200
    21→
    22→
    23→def cap_qwen_resolution(
    24→    resolution_str: str,
    25→    max_dimension: int = QWEN_MAX_DIMENSION,
    26→    task_id: str = "unknown",
    27→) -> Optional[str]:
    28→    """
    29→    Cap resolution to max_dimension px on the longest side while maintaining aspect ratio.
    30→
    31→    Returns the (possibly capped) resolution string, or None if the input is invalid.
    32→    """
    33→    if not resolution_str or "x" not in resolution_str:
    34→        return None
    35→    try:
    36→        width, height = map(int, resolution_str.split("x"))
    37→    except ValueError:
    38→        model_logger.warning(
    39→            f"[QWEN_COMPOSITOR] Task {task_id}: Invalid resolution format: {resolution_str}",
    40→            task_id=task_id,
    41→        )
    42→        return None
    43→
    44→    if width > max_dimension or height > max_dimension:
    45→        ratio = min(max_dimension / width, max_dimension / height)
    46→        width = int(width * ratio)
    47→        height = int(height * ratio)
    48→        capped = f"{width}x{height}"
    49→        model_logger.info(
    50→            f"[QWEN_COMPOSITOR] Task {task_id}: Resolution capped from {resolution_str} to {capped}",
    51→            task_id=task_id,
    52→        )
    53→        return capped
    54→    return resolution_str
    55→
    56→
    57→def create_qwen_masked_composite(
    58→    image_url: str,
    59→    mask_url: str,
    60→    output_dir: Path,
    61→    task_id: str = "unknown",
    62→) -> str:
    63→    """
    64→    Create composite image with green overlay for Qwen inpainting/annotation.
    65→
    66→    Downloads the source image and binary mask, resizes both to fit within
    67→    QWEN_MAX_DIMENSION, applies a green overlay on masked regions, and saves
    68→    the result as JPEG.
    69→
    70→    Returns the path to the saved composite image.
    71→
    72→    Raises ValueError if compositing fails.
    73→    """
    74→    try:
    75→        model_logger.debug(f"[QWEN_COMPOSITOR] Task {task_id}: Downloading image from {image_url}", task_id=task_id)
    76→        img_response = requests.get(image_url, timeout=30)
    77→        img_response.raise_for_status()
    78→        image = Image.open(BytesIO(img_response.content)).convert("RGB")
    79→
    80→        max_dimension = QWEN_MAX_DIMENSION
    81→        width, height = image.size
    82→        if width > max_dimension or height > max_dimension:
    83→            ratio = min(max_dimension / width, max_dimension / height)
    84→            new_width = int(width * ratio)
    85→            new_height = int(height * ratio)
    86→            image = image.resize((new_width, new_height), Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)
    87→            model_logger.debug(f"[QWEN_COMPOSITOR] Task {task_id}: Resized image from {width}x{height} to {new_width}x{new_height}", task_id=task_id)
    88→            width, height = new_width, new_height
    89→
    90→        model_logger.debug(f"[QWEN_COMPOSITOR] Task {task_id}: Downloading mask from {mask_url}", task_id=task_id)
    91→        mask_response = requests.get(mask_url, timeout=30)
    92→        mask_response.raise_for_status()
    93→        mask = Image.open(BytesIO(mask_response.content)).convert("L")
    94→
    95→        if mask.size != (width, height):
    96→            mask = mask.resize((width, height), Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)
    97→            model_logger.debug(f"[QWEN_COMPOSITOR] Task {task_id}: Resized mask to match image: {width}x{height}", task_id=task_id)
    98→
    99→        mask = mask.point(lambda x: 0 if x < 128 else 255)
   100→        green_overlay = Image.new("RGB", (width, height), (0, 255, 0))
   101→        composite = Image.composite(green_overlay, image, mask)
   102→
   103→        output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   104→        composite_filename = f"inpaint_composite_{task_id}.jpg"
   105→        composite_path = output_dir / composite_filename
   106→        composite.save(composite_path, "JPEG", quality=95)
   107→
   108→        model_logger.info(
   109→            f"[QWEN_COMPOSITOR] Task {task_id}: Created green mask composite: {composite_path}",
   110→            task_id=task_id,
   111→        )
   112→        return str(composite_path)
   113→    except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
   114→        model_logger.error(
   115→            f"[QWEN_COMPOSITOR] Task {task_id}: Failed to create masked composite: {e}",
   116→            task_id=task_id,
   117→        )
   118→        raise ValueError(f"Composite image creation failed: {e}")
   119→
```

```
1→"""
     2→TaskConfig - the main typed configuration object.
     3→
     4→This combines all parameter groups and handles:
     5→- Parsing from DB tasks
     6→- Parsing from segment parameters
     7→- Converting to WGP format
     8→"""
     9→
    10→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    11→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Callable
    12→import logging
    13→
    14→from .base import ParamGroup
    15→from .lora import LoRAConfig
    16→from .vace import VACEConfig
    17→from .generation import GenerationConfig
    18→from .phase import PhaseConfig
    19→
    20→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    21→
    22→
    23→@dataclass
    24→class TaskConfig(ParamGroup):
    25→    """
    26→    Complete task configuration combining all parameter groups.
    27→    
    28→    This is the main entry point for typed parameter handling.
    29→    """
    30→    generation: GenerationConfig = field(default_factory=GenerationConfig)
    31→    lora: LoRAConfig = field(default_factory=LoRAConfig)
    32→    vace: VACEConfig = field(default_factory=VACEConfig)
    33→    phase: PhaseConfig = field(default_factory=PhaseConfig)
    34→    
    35→    # Task metadata
    36→    task_id: str = ""
    37→    task_type: str = ""
    38→    model: str = ""
    39→    
    40→    # Extra params that don't fit elsewhere (passthrough)
    41→    extra_params: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    42→    
    43→    @classmethod
    44→    def from_params(cls, params: Dict[str, Any], **context) -> 'TaskConfig':
    45→        """
    46→        Parse TaskConfig from a flat parameter dict.
    47→        
    48→        Use from_db_task() for DB tasks that need flattening first.
    49→        """
    50→        task_id = context.get('task_id', '')
    51→        task_type = context.get('task_type', '')
    52→        model = context.get('model', '') or params.get('model_name', '')
    53→        
    54→        # Parse each component
    55→        generation = GenerationConfig.from_params(params, **context)
    56→        vace = VACEConfig.from_params(params, **context)
    57→        phase = PhaseConfig.from_params(params, **context)
    58→        
    59→        # Parse LoRA config
    60→        lora = LoRAConfig.from_params(params, **context)
    61→        
    62→        # If phase config has LoRAs, merge them in
    63→        if not phase.is_empty():
    64→            lora_info = phase.get_lora_info()
    65→            if lora_info.get('additional_loras'):
    66→                phase_lora = LoRAConfig.from_params({
    67→                    'additional_loras': lora_info['additional_loras'],
    68→                    'lora_names': lora_info.get('lora_names', []),
    69→                    'lora_multipliers': lora_info.get('lora_multipliers', []),
    70→                }, **context)
    71→                lora = lora.merge(phase_lora)
    72→        
    73→        # Collect extra params (everything not handled by specific groups)
    74→        handled_keys = {
    75→            'prompt', 'negative_prompt', 'resolution', 'video_length',
    76→            'num_inference_steps', 'steps', 'guidance_scale', 'seed',
    77→            'flow_shift', 'sample_solver', 'image_start', 'image_end', 'image_refs',
    78→            'embedded_guidance_scale', 'denoising_strength', 'denoise_strength',
    79→            'video_guide', 'video_mask', 'video_prompt_type',
    80→            'control_net_weight', 'control_net_weight2',
    81→            'activated_loras', 'lora_names', 'loras_multipliers', 'lora_multipliers',
    82→            'additional_loras', 'phase_config', 'model_name',
    83→            # Image-to-image parameter aliases
    84→            'image', 'image_url', 'strength',
    85→        }
    86→        extra_params = {k: v for k, v in params.items() if k not in handled_keys}
    87→        
    88→        return cls(
    89→            generation=generation,
    90→            lora=lora,
    91→            vace=vace,
    92→            phase=phase,
    93→            task_id=task_id,
    94→            task_type=task_type,
    95→            model=model,
    96→            extra_params=extra_params,
    97→        )
    98→    
    99→    @classmethod
   100→    def from_db_task(cls, db_params: Dict[str, Any], **context) -> 'TaskConfig':
   101→        """
   102→        Parse TaskConfig from DB task parameters.
   103→        
   104→        Handles nested orchestrator_payload/orchestrator_details with proper precedence.
   105→        """
   106→        # Flatten with precedence
   107→        flat_params = ParamGroup.flatten_params(db_params, context.get('task_id', ''))
   108→        return cls.from_params(flat_params, **context)
   109→    
   110→    @classmethod
   111→    def from_segment_params(
   112→        cls,
   113→        segment_params: Dict[str, Any],
   114→        orchestrator_payload: Dict[str, Any],
   115→        individual_params: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
   116→        **context
   117→    ) -> 'TaskConfig':
   118→        """
   119→        Parse TaskConfig from segment parameters with proper precedence.
   120→        
   121→        Precedence: individual_params > segment_params > orchestrator_payload
   122→        """
   123→        # Build merged params with precedence
   124→        merged = {}
   125→        
   126→        # Start with orchestrator_payload (lowest)
   127→        if orchestrator_payload:
   128→            merged.update(orchestrator_payload)
   129→        
   130→        # Then segment_params (medium)
   131→        if segment_params:
   132→            merged.update(segment_params)
   133→        
   134→        # Finally individual_params (highest)
   135→        if individual_params:
   136→            merged.update(individual_params)
   137→        
   138→        return cls.from_params(merged, **context)
   139→    
   140→    def to_wgp_format(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   141→        """
   142→        Convert to WGP-compatible parameter dict.
   143→        
   144→        This is the single point of conversion to WGP format.
   145→        """
   146→        result = {}
   147→        
   148→        # Add generation params
   149→        result.update(self.generation.to_wgp_format())
   150→        
   151→        # Add LoRA params
   152→        result.update(self.lora.to_wgp_format())
   153→        
   154→        # CRITICAL: Also include pending downloads so downstream can handle them
   155→        if self.lora.has_pending_downloads():
   156→            result['additional_loras'] = self.lora.get_pending_downloads()
   157→        
   158→        # Add VACE params
   159→        result.update(self.vace.to_wgp_format())
   160→        
   161→        # Add phase config params
   162→        if not self.phase.is_empty():
   163→            phase_params = self.phase.to_wgp_format()
   164→            
   165→            # Don't duplicate LoRA params from phase (already handled above)
   166→            for key in ['lora_names', 'lora_multipliers', 'additional_loras']:
   167→                phase_params.pop(key, None)
   168→            
   169→            result.update(phase_params)
   170→            
   171→            # If phase config requires patching, pass the info through
   172→            if self.phase.has_patch_config():
   173→                result['_parsed_phase_config'] = self.phase.parsed_output
   174→                result['_phase_config_model_name'] = self.phase.patch_model_name or self.model
   175→        
   176→        # Add model
   177→        if self.model:
   178→            result['model'] = self.model
   179→        
   180→        # Add extra params (passthrough)
   181→        result.update(self.extra_params)
   182→        
   183→        return result
   184→    
   185→    def validate(self) -> List[str]:
   186→        """Validate entire task configuration."""
   187→        errors = []
   188→        
   189→        errors.extend(self.generation.validate())
   190→        errors.extend(self.lora.validate())
   191→        errors.extend(self.vace.validate())
   192→        errors.extend(self.phase.validate())
   193→        
   194→        # Check LoRA/multiplier count match
   195→        lora_format = self.lora.to_wgp_format()
   196→        lora_count = len(lora_format.get('activated_loras', []))
   197→        mult_str = lora_format.get('loras_multipliers', '')
   198→        
   199→        if lora_count > 0 and mult_str:
   200→            mult_str_stripped = mult_str.strip()
   201→            if mult_str_stripped:
   202→                # Count multipliers based on format
   203→                if ';' in mult_str_stripped:
   204→                    mult_count = len(mult_str_stripped.split())
   205→                elif ',' in mult_str_stripped:
   206→                    mult_count = len(mult_str_stripped.split(','))
   207→                else:
   208→                    mult_count = len(mult_str_stripped.split())
   209→                
   210→                if mult_count != lora_count:
   211→                    errors.append(f"LoRA count ({lora_count}) != multiplier count ({mult_count})")
   212→        
   213→        return errors
   214→    
   215→    def log_summary(self, log_func: Callable = None):
   216→        """Log a summary of the configuration."""
   217→        if log_func is None:

   226→        if not self.phase.is_empty():
```

```
23→def validate_lora_file(file_path: Path, filename: str) -> tuple[bool, str]:
    24→    """
    25→    Validates a LoRA file for size and format integrity.
    26→
    27→    Returns:
    28→        (is_valid, error_message)
    29→    """
    30→    if not file_path.exists():
    31→        return False, f"File does not exist: {file_path}"
    32→
    33→    file_size = file_path.stat().st_size
    34→
    35→    # Known LoRA size ranges (in bytes)
    36→    # These are based on common LoRA architectures and rank sizes
    37→    LORA_SIZE_RANGES = {
    38→        # Very small LoRAs (rank 4-8)
    39→        'tiny': (1_000_000, 50_000_000),      # 1MB - 50MB
    40→        # Standard LoRAs (rank 16-32)
    41→        'standard': (50_000_000, 500_000_000),  # 50MB - 500MB
    42→        # Large LoRAs (rank 64+) or full model fine-tunes
    43→        'large': (500_000_000, 5_000_000_000), # 500MB - 5GB
    44→        # Extremely large (full model weights)
    45→        'xlarge': (5_000_000_000, 50_000_000_000)  # 5GB - 50GB
    46→    }
    47→
    48→    # Check if file size is within any reasonable range
    49→    in_valid_range = any(
    50→        min_size <= file_size <= max_size
    51→        for min_size, max_size in LORA_SIZE_RANGES.values()
    52→    )
    53→
    54→    if not in_valid_range:
    55→        if file_size < 1_000_000:  # Less than 1MB
    56→            return False, f"File too small ({file_size:,} bytes) - likely corrupted or incomplete download"
    57→        elif file_size > 50_000_000_000:  # More than 50GB
    58→            return False, f"File too large ({file_size:,} bytes) - likely not a LoRA file"
    59→
    60→    # For safetensors files, try to open and inspect
    61→    if filename.endswith('.safetensors'):
    62→        try:
    63→            import safetensors.torch as st
    64→            with st.safe_open(file_path, framework="pt") as f:
    65→                keys = list(f.keys())
    66→
    67→                # LoRAs typically have keys like "lora_down.weight", "lora_up.weight", etc.
    68→                lora_indicators = ['lora_down', 'lora_up', 'lora.down', 'lora.up', 'lora_A', 'lora_B']
    69→                has_lora_keys = any(indicator in key for key in keys for indicator in lora_indicators)
    70→
    71→                if not has_lora_keys and len(keys) > LORA_FULL_MODEL_TENSOR_THRESHOLD:
    72→                    # Might be a full model checkpoint rather than a LoRA
    73→                    headless_logger.warning(f"{filename} appears to be a full model checkpoint ({len(keys)} tensors) rather than a LoRA")
    74→                elif not has_lora_keys:
    75→                    headless_logger.warning(f"{filename} doesn't appear to contain LoRA weights (no lora_* keys found)")
    76→
    77→                # Check for reasonable number of parameters
    78→                if len(keys) == 0:
    79→                    return False, "Safetensors file contains no tensors"
    80→                elif len(keys) > LORA_EXCESSIVE_TENSOR_THRESHOLD:
    81→                    headless_logger.warning(f"{filename} contains many tensors ({len(keys)}) - might be a full model")
    82→
    83→        except ImportError:
    84→            headless_logger.debug(f"[WARNING] safetensors not available for detailed validation of {filename}")
    85→        except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
    86→            return False, f"Safetensors file appears corrupted: {e}"
    87→
    88→    # Additional checks for common corruption patterns
    89→    if file_size == 0:
    90→        return False, "File is empty"
    91→
    92→    # For binary files, check they don't start with common error HTML patterns
    93→    try:
    94→        with open(file_path, 'rb') as f:
    95→            first_bytes = f.read(HTML_SNIFF_BYTES)
    96→            if first_bytes.startswith(b'<!DOCTYPE html') or first_bytes.startswith(b'<html'):
    97→                return False, "File appears to be an HTML error page rather than a LoRA file"
    98→    except OSError as e:
    99→        headless_logger.warning(f"Could not read first bytes of {filename} for HTML check: {e}")
   100→
   101→    return True, f"File validated successfully ({file_size:,} bytes)"
   102→
```

All 51 tests pass. Now let me do a thorough analysis of each test file against its source code.

Here is my analysis of all problems found across the 5 test files:

---

## 1. `test_task_status.py` -- `TestRequeueTaskForRetry::test_increments_attempts`

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_task_status.py`, lines 49-59

**What's wrong:** The test patches `_call_edge_function_with_retry` but then inspects `mock_edge.call_args` -- however `mock_edge` is the return value of the `patch()` context manager used as `return_value=_edge_ok()`, not the mock itself. The `with patch(...) as mock_edge` captures the patcher's mock, which IS the correct mock for `_call_edge_function_with_retry`. So the mock is correct here.

However, the test asserts `payload["attempts"] == 3` but does NOT verify that `payload["status"]` is `"Queued"` or that `payload["clear_worker"]` is `True`. If the production code stopped setting those critical fields, this test would still pass. The assertion is too narrow -- it only checks one out of several important payload fields.

**Fix:** Add assertions for `payload["status"] == "Queued"` and `payload["clear_worker"] is True` and `payload["task_id"] == "task-1"`.

---

## 2. `test_task_status.py` -- `TestRequeueTaskForRetry::test_includes_error_category`

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_task_status.py`, lines 61-70

**What's wrong:** The assertion `assert "OOM" in payload["error_details"]` is a very weak substring check. The production code builds `error_details` as `f"Retry {new_attempts} ({error_category}): {error_message[:500]}"`. The test only checks that "OOM" appears somewhere in the string. If the production code dropped the error_category and the error_message happened to contain "OOM", the test would still pass. More importantly, if the production code changed to put error_category in a different field, this test wouldn't catch it.

**Fix:** Assert the structure more precisely, e.g. `assert payload["error_details"].startswith("Retry 1 (OOM)")` or at minimum assert the error_category appears between parentheses.

---

## 3. `test_task_status.py` -- `TestRequeueTaskForRetry::test_success_via_edge`

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_task_status.py`, lines 28-34

**What's wrong:** This test only verifies the return value is `True`. It does not verify that `_call_edge_function_with_retry` was actually called, nor what arguments it was called with. If the production code short-circuited and returned `True` without ever calling the edge function (e.g., a caching bug), this test would still pass.

**Fix:** Capture the mock and assert it was called exactly once, and optionally check the URL or payload.

---

## 4. `test_task_status.py` -- `TestRequeueTaskForRetry::test_fallback_to_direct_db`

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_task_status.py`, lines 36-47

**What's wrong:** The test verifies that `True` is returned when the edge function fails and the direct DB path succeeds. But it does not verify that the direct DB `.update()` call was actually made, or that the update payload contains the correct fields (status="Queued", worker_id=None, attempts=2, etc.). If the production code returned `True` without actually performing the DB update, this test would still pass.

**Fix:** Assert that `mock_db_config["client"].table(...).update(...)` was called and inspect the update dict for correct fields.

---

## 5. `test_task_completion.py` -- `TestAddTaskToDb::test_success_returns_uuid`

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_task_completion.py`, lines 24-40

**What's wrong:** The test asserts `isinstance(task_id, str)` and `len(task_id) == 36`, which only validates the format of `uuid.uuid4()`. Since `add_task_to_db` generates the UUID internally and always returns it on success, these assertions verify Python's `uuid` module works, not that the production code is correct. The test does NOT verify:
- That `_call_edge_function_with_retry` was called with the correct payload structure (task_id, params, task_type, project_id, dependant_on)
- That the task_type was passed correctly
- That the project_id was extracted from params

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_task_completion.py`, lines 59-77

```python

```

Looking at how `_call_edge_function_with_retry` is called in the source (line 118-125 of `task_completion.py`), the `payload` is a positional argument (second arg). But the mock's `call_args` structure depends on whether it was called with positional or keyword args. If the source changes to pass `payload=` as a keyword, `call_kwargs[0][1]` would break silently because the `or` fallback would kick in. This same fragile extraction pattern appears in `test_task_status.py` test_increments_attempts and test_includes_error_category.

**Fix:** Use a more robust extraction. Since the source always passes payload positionally, assert with `call_kwargs[0][1]` directly and remove the fallback, OR always use the keyword form. The current code works but masks potential future breakage.

## 8. `test_task_dependencies.py` -- `TestGetTaskCurrentStatus::test_fallback_to_direct`

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_task_dependencies.py`, lines 191-201

**What's wrong:** The mock chain is:
```python
mock_db_config["client"].table.return_value.select.return_value.eq.return_value.single.return_value.execute.return_value = mock_resp
```

But `mock_resp.data = {"status": "Queued"}` sets `.data` to a dict. Looking at the source code (line 244-246), `get_task_current_status` does:
```python
resp = _cfg.SUPABASE_CLIENT.table(...).select("status").eq("id", task_id).single().execute()
if resp.data:
    return resp.data.get("status")
```

This works because `MagicMock` chains always return new `MagicMock` objects, so `.table.return_value.select.return_value.eq.return_value` matches `.table(...).select(...).eq(...)` regardless of the arguments passed. The test would pass even if the production code queried the wrong table name, wrong column, or wrong task ID. This is a general problem with deeply-chained Supabase mock setups across all tests that use `mock_db_config["client"]`.

**Fix:** Either use `assert_called_with` on intermediate calls, or at minimum verify `.table` was called with the correct table name and `.eq` was called with `("id", "task-1")`.

## 11. `test_task_completion.py` -- `TestAddTaskToDb::test_raises_on_none_response`

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_task_completion.py`, lines 94-100

**What's wrong:** The test mocks `_call_edge_function_with_retry` to return `(None, None)`. Looking at the source (lines 128-131):

```python
if edge_error:
    raise RuntimeError(f"Edge Function create-task failed: {edge_error}")
if resp is None:
    raise RuntimeError(f"Edge Function create-task returned no response for {actual_db_row_id}")
```

The `(None, None)` return skips the `if edge_error` check (since `None` is falsy) and hits the `if resp is None` check. This is testing the correct code path. However, `_call_edge_function_with_retry` can never actually return `(None, None)` in practice -- it always returns either `(response, None)` on success or `(None_or_response, error_string)` on failure. The test is verifying behavior for an impossible input.

**Fix:** This test is defensive (testing impossible state), which is not necessarily wrong, but it might be better to test a realistic failure case instead, like `(None, "TIMEOUT")`.

```python
    ...

else:
    ...
    return None
```

The test is checking that non-Complete status returns None. But the `status` is checked against `STATUS_COMPLETE` which is defined in config. The test data uses the raw string `"In Progress"`. If `STATUS_COMPLETE` was changed to something unexpected, the test would still pass (because any non-matching status returns None). This test would pass even if the status comparison was completely broken -- for example, if the code was `if False:` this test would still return None.

**Fix:** Add a complementary test that verifies Complete status WITH output_location DOES return the output_location (which `test_success_returns_output` already does, so this specific concern is mitigated). But a better test would be to verify the status check is actually happening -- e.g., test that Complete status with output_location returns the value (already covered) and that Complete status WITHOUT output_location returns None.

## Test Review: Issues Found

### 1. `test_lora_validation.py` -- `TestValidateLoraFile::test_html_error_page`

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_lora_validation.py`, line 37-42

**What's wrong:** The mock on `safetensors.torch.safe_open` makes this test pass for the wrong reason. The test creates a file that starts with `<!DOCTYPE html>` but is padded to 2,000,000 bytes (which puts it in the `tiny` valid-size range). The production code runs the safetensors check *before* the HTML sniff check (lines 61-86 run before lines 93-97 in the source). The mock makes `safe_open` succeed with fake LoRA keys, so the safetensors block passes silently, and execution continues to the HTML byte-sniff which correctly catches it. 

However, this test would **also pass if the HTML sniff code was deleted** -- but only because a real `.safetensors` file full of HTML would fail the actual `safe_open()` call with an exception (caught on line 85-86), returning `False` with "appears corrupted". The mock suppresses that natural failure, making the test depend on the HTML sniff path surviving. If someone changed the HTML sniff code path (or reordered code), this mock would mask the problem.

**The deeper issue:** The mock prevents testing the *actual* safetensors-parsing behavior on HTML content. In reality, `safe_open()` on an HTML file would raise an exception, and the function would return `(False, "Safetensors file appears corrupted: ...")` before ever reaching the HTML byte sniff. The test asserts `"HTML" in msg`, but in production the message would be about corruption, not HTML. The test is verifying a code path that can never actually be reached for `.safetensors` files with HTML content.

**Fix:** Either (a) remove the mock and let `safe_open` fail naturally, then assert `"corrupted" in msg` to test what actually happens, or (b) test the HTML sniff with a non-`.safetensors` extension like `.bin` (where the safetensors block is skipped), or (c) split into two tests -- one for `.safetensors` HTML content (no mock, expect corruption message) and one for `.bin` HTML content (expect HTML message).

## 1. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_worker_integration.py`

### Problem 1: `test_worker_importable` is permanently xfail and never re-checked

**Test:** `TestWorkerImports.test_worker_importable` (line 127-129)

**What's wrong:** The test is marked `@pytest.mark.xfail(reason="edit_video_orchestrator.py has uncommitted syntax error")`. This means it will silently pass whether it actually succeeds or fails. If the syntax error was fixed, the test would still be marked xfail and you'd never know the import works again (or notice if it breaks in a new way). The xfail reason references "uncommitted" changes, suggesting this was meant to be temporary.

**Fix:** Either remove the xfail marker now (since the file exists in the modified list and presumably works), or change to `@pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` so that if the import starts working, the test suite will notify you to remove the marker.

### Problem 2: `test_params_set_correctly` has weak assertions on critical LoRA output

**Test:** `TestQwenImageEditHandler.test_params_set_correctly` (line 61-87)

**What's wrong:** The test asserts `len(generation_params["lora_names"]) > 0` (line 87) but doesn't verify the actual LoRA filename that was added. Since the test mocks `hf_hub_download` and touches a fake LoRA file, the handler's `_add_lightning_lora` will append the lightning LoRA filename. But the assertion would pass even if the wrong LoRA was added or if two LoRAs were added when only one was expected. Similarly, line 83 asserts `"num_inference_steps" in generation_params` but doesn't check its value (should be 12).

**Fix:** Assert the specific expected LoRA filename (`[REDACTED]` -- wait, actually looking at the source, the lightning LoRA uses the variant config). More importantly, assert `len(generation_params["lora_names"]) == 1` and check that the value is the expected lightning LoRA filename. Assert `generation_params["num_inference_steps"] == 12`.

### Problem 3: `test_style_params` doesn't verify LoRA multiplier/strength values

**Test:** `TestQwenImageStyleHandler.test_style_params` (line 95-121)

**What's wrong:** The test passes `style_reference_strength=0.8` but only asserts `len(generation_params.get("lora_names", [])) > 0`. It doesn't verify that the style LoRA was added with the correct multiplier (0.8), or that the lightning LoRA was added with its expected strength. The mock for `hf_hub_download` returns a path but the function under test calls `_add_task_lora` which calls `_download_lora_if_missing` -- but wait, the `.touch()` calls on line 100-105 create the files, so the download won't be attempted. However, the lora `style_transfer_qwen_edit_2_000011250.safetensors` is touched on disk but `_add_task_lora` calls `_download_lora_if_missing` which checks `(self.qwen_lora_dir / filename).exists()`. Since the test touches the file there, it should be found. The real issue is that the assertion `len(...) > 0` would pass with just the lightning LoRA -- the style LoRA might not be added at all and the test would still pass.

**Fix:** Assert the expected count of LoRAs (should be 2: lightning + style) and verify the style LoRA filename is in the list. Also verify that the style LoRA's multiplier is 0.8.
