## 3. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_centralized_modules.py`

### Problem 1: `test_get_default_model_known_types` is tautological

**Test:** `TestTaskTypes.test_get_default_model_known_types` (line 58-65)

**What's wrong:** Lines 63-64 iterate over `TASK_TYPE_TO_MODEL.items()` and assert `get_default_model(task_type) == expected_model`. But looking at the source, `get_default_model` is almost certainly just `return TASK_TYPE_TO_MODEL.get(task_type, "t2v")`. So this test is asserting that a dict lookup returns the value from the same dict -- it's a tautology. The test on line 65 (`assert get_default_model("flux") == "flux"`) is the only one with a hardcoded expected value, which is actually useful.

**Fix:** Replace the loop with hardcoded expected values for a few important task types (e.g., `assert get_default_model("vace") == "wan_2_1_vace"`, etc.). This way the test catches regressions where someone accidentally changes the mapping.

### Problem 2: `test_apply_all_wgp_patches_returns_dict` tests mock behavior, not real behavior

**Test:** `TestWgpPatches.test_apply_all_wgp_patches_returns_dict` (line 211-228)

**What's wrong:** The test creates a `ModuleType("mock_wgp")` with stub lambdas and passes it to `apply_all_wgp_patches`. The test only verifies the return value is a dict with certain keys. It doesn't verify that any actual patching occurred -- i.e., it doesn't check that `mock_wgp.load_wan_model` was replaced or that any monkey-patches were applied. The function could return `{"qwen_model_routing": False, "qwen_lora_directory": False}` (indicating patches failed) and the test would still pass.

**Fix:** Assert that the result dict values are truthy (patches succeeded), and optionally check that `mock_wgp.load_wan_model` is no longer the original lambda (was actually patched).

### Problem 3: `test_db_operations_uses_httpx` inspects source code instead of behavior

**Test:** `TestPhase1BugFixes.test_db_operations_uses_httpx` (line 263-271)

**What's wrong:** This test uses `inspect.getsource(edge_helpers)` and checks for the string `"import httpx"` in the source. This is a fragile approach -- if someone changes the import to `from httpx import Client` or `import httpx as http`, the test would fail even though httpx is still being used. Conversely, if someone adds `import httpx` in a comment but uses requests, the test passes.

**Fix:** This is a minor style issue. A slightly better approach would be to check `hasattr(edge_helpers, 'httpx')` or just check that `import requests` is NOT in the source. But the test is catching a real historical regression (switching from requests to httpx), so the practical risk is low.

The second operand `"phase_config" in self.TRAVEL_SEGMENT_PARAMS` is **always True** because `TRAVEL_SEGMENT_PARAMS` is defined with a `"phase_config"` key on line 320. So this assertion can never fail regardless of what `to_wgp_format()` returns. The test is checking nothing.

The mock is set up with `side_effect = lambda url, task_id: os.path.basename(url)` which just returns the basename. But the test then calls `config.lora.mark_downloaded(url, local_path)` directly. This doesn't actually test `_download_lora_from_url` at all -- it tests `mark_downloaded`. The `with patch(...)` context manager is unnecessary since the patched function's behavior is just `os.path.basename(url)`, which is what the test manually invokes.

**Fix:** Either remove the mock entirely and just use `os.path.basename(url)` directly (making it clearer this is testing the config/format pipeline, not the download step), or actually test `_download_lora_from_url` with its real logic (which is what `TestDownloaderUtils` already does better).

### Problem 1: `test_single_config_full_coverage` doesn't verify output frame count

**Test:** `TestCompositeGuidanceVideo.test_single_config_full_coverage` (line 209-231)

**What's wrong:** The test only asserts `result.exists()` and `result.stat().st_size > 0`. It doesn't verify the output video has the expected number of frames (20). Compare with `test_multiple_configs_with_gap` (line 234-264) which does verify frame count. The production code could output a 1-frame video or a 100-frame video and this test would still pass.

**Fix:** Add frame count verification:
```python
import cv2
cap = cv2.VideoCapture(str(result))
frame_count = int(cap.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_COUNT))
cap.release()
assert frame_count == 20
```

### Problem 2: `test_source_range_extraction` and `test_different_treatments` have no meaningful assertions

The `test_different_treatments` test specifies `"treatment": "adjust"` vs `"treatment": "clip"` but never checks that different treatments produced different results.

**Fix:** For `test_source_range_extraction`, verify the frame count is 20. For `test_different_treatments`, at minimum verify frame count (40). Ideally, compare pixel content to verify the correct source frames were used, though this may be overkill for a unit test.

### Problem 3: `test_segment_with_full_overlap` and `test_segment_with_partial_overlap` don't verify frame content

**Test:** `TestExtractSegmentStructureGuidance.test_segment_with_full_overlap` (line 474-502) and `test_segment_with_partial_overlap` (line 504-540)

**Fix:** Add frame count verification to `test_segment_with_full_overlap`:

## Test Analysis: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_edge_helpers.py`

### Problem 1: `test_404_with_fallback_url` — Does not verify the fallback URL was actually used

**What's wrong:** The test sets `side_effect` to `[404_response, 200_response]` and provides a `fallback_url`, then asserts success. However, the mock doesn't distinguish between URLs. If the fallback logic was broken — e.g., if it retried the *original* URL instead of the fallback URL — the second mock call would still return 200 and the test would still pass. The test proves that two HTTP calls were made and the second succeeded, but not that the second call went to `fallback_url`.

**Fix:** Assert that the second call to `mock_httpx["post"]` was made with the fallback URL:

```python
def test_404_with_fallback_url(self, mock_httpx, mock_sleep):
    mock_httpx["post"].side_effect = [
        _err_response(404, "not found"),
        _ok_response({"fallback": True}),
    ]
    resp, err = _call_edge_function_with_retry(
        URL, {}, HEADERS, "test-fn",
        fallback_url="https://edge.test/fn-fallback",
    )
    assert err is None
    assert resp.status_code == 200
    # Verify fallback URL was actually used
    calls = mock_httpx["post"].call_args_list
    assert calls[0].args[0] == URL  # original URL first
    assert calls[1].args[0] == "https://edge.test/fn-fallback"  # fallback URL second
```

---

### Problem 2: `test_404_with_retryable_pattern` — Indistinguishable from a plain 5xx retry

**What's wrong:** The test uses `max_retries=2` with `side_effect = [404 "Task not found", 200]` and `retry_on_404_patterns=["Task not found"]`. It only asserts `err is None`. But consider: if the 404-pattern retry logic was completely deleted from production code, the function would fall through to the generic non-retryable error path (`HTTP_404`) and return an error. So the test does catch complete removal. However, it does NOT distinguish between the 404-pattern-matching retry and a hypothetical bug where ALL 404s get retried unconditionally. There's no assertion that a 404 *without* the matching pattern would NOT be retried. The test also doesn't verify call count or that backoff happened.

**Fix:** Add a companion test that verifies a 404 without a matching pattern is NOT retried, and verify call count on the existing test:

```python
def test_404_with_retryable_pattern(self, mock_httpx, mock_sleep):
    mock_httpx["post"].side_effect = [
        _err_response(404, "Task not found"),
        _ok_response(),
    ]
    resp, err = _call_edge_function_with_retry(
        URL, {}, HEADERS, "test-fn",
        max_retries=2,
        retry_on_404_patterns=["Task not found"],
    )
    assert err is None
    assert mock_httpx["post"].call_count == 2

def test_404_without_matching_pattern_not_retried(self, mock_httpx, mock_sleep):
    mock_httpx["post"].return_value = _err_response(404, "Something else entirely")
    resp, err = _call_edge_function_with_retry(
        URL, {}, HEADERS, "test-fn",
        max_retries=3,
        retry_on_404_patterns=["Task not found"],
    )
    assert err is not None
    assert "HTTP_404" in err
    assert mock_httpx["post"].call_count == 1  # no retry
```

---

### Problem 3: `test_put_with_file_path` — Does not verify the file-reading branch was taken

**What's wrong:** The test creates a real file via `tmp_path`, passes its path as a string, and asserts `err is None`. But it never verifies that `httpx.put` was called with a file handle (the `content` kwarg being an open file object) rather than being called with the string path directly. If the `isinstance(payload, (str, Path)) and Path(payload).exists()` check was broken and it fell through to the `else` branch (passing the raw string as `content`), this test would still pass because the mock doesn't care what `content` is.

**Fix:** Assert that `httpx.put` was called with a file-like object as `content`, or at minimum assert the call was made with the expected kwargs:

```python
def test_put_with_file_path(self, mock_httpx, mock_sleep, tmp_path):
    f = tmp_path / "upload.bin"
    f.write_bytes(b"binary-data")
    mock_httpx["put"].return_value = _ok_response()
    resp, err = _call_edge_function_with_retry(
        URL, str(f), HEADERS, "upload", method="PUT",
    )
    assert err is None
    mock_httpx["put"].assert_called_once()
    # Verify content was a file handle, not the raw string path
    call_kwargs = mock_httpx["put"].call_args.kwargs
    assert hasattr(call_kwargs["content"], "read"), \
        "Expected a file handle for content, not a raw string"
```

**Caveat:** Since the production code uses a `with` block, the file handle is closed by the time we inspect the call args. This means `hasattr(..., "read")` will still work (closed files still have a `read` attribute), but reading from it would fail. An alternative approach is to use a side_effect on the mock to capture and read the content while the file is still open.

---

### Problem 4: `test_retry_on_503`, `test_retry_on_504`, `test_retry_on_500` — Missing call count assertions

**What's wrong:** Unlike `test_retry_on_502` which asserts `mock_httpx["post"].call_count == 2`, these three retry tests only assert `err is None`. While `err is None` does prove that a retry happened (since success can only come from the second `side_effect` entry), these tests don't verify that exactly 2 calls were made. If the production code had a bug causing 3 or more calls before reaching the success response, these tests wouldn't catch it because `side_effect` with a list raises `StopIteration` after exhaustion, which would manifest as a different failure mode. More importantly, the inconsistency with `test_retry_on_502` looks like an oversight.

**Fix:** Add `assert mock_httpx["post"].call_count == 2` to each of these tests, matching the pattern in `test_retry_on_502`.

---

### Problem 5: No test verifies exponential backoff sleep durations

**What's wrong:** The `mock_sleep` fixture patches `time.sleep` but doesn't yield the mock, so tests cannot assert on it. The production code uses exponential backoff (`wait_time = 2 ** attempt`), but no test verifies that `time.sleep` was called with the correct values (1, 2, 4, etc.). The retry logic's backoff could be changed to a constant, removed entirely, or set to absurdly large values, and all tests would still pass.

**Fix:** Update the `mock_sleep` fixture to yield the mock object, then add assertions in retry tests:

In `conftest.py`:
```python
@pytest.fixture
def mock_sleep():
    """Patch ``time.sleep`` to no-op for retry tests."""
    with patch("time.sleep") as mock:
        yield mock
```

Then in a retry test (e.g., `test_max_retries_exhausted`):
```python
def test_max_retries_exhausted(self, mock_httpx, mock_sleep):
    mock_httpx["post"].return_value = _err_response(502, "bad gateway")
    resp, err = _call_edge_function_with_retry(URL, {}, HEADERS, "test-fn", max_retries=3)
    assert err is not None
    assert "5XX_TRANSIENT" in err
    assert mock_httpx["post"].call_count == 3
    # Verify exponential backoff: 2^0=1, 2^1=2 (only 2 sleeps for 3 attempts)
    assert mock_sleep.call_count == 2
    mock_sleep.assert_any_call(1)  # attempt 0: 2**0
    mock_sleep.assert_any_call(2)  # attempt 1: 2**1
```

---

| 1 | `test_404_with_fallback_url` | **High** | Would pass even if fallback URL was never used (just retried original URL) |
| 2 | `test_404_with_retryable_pattern` | **Medium** | No companion test proving non-matching 404s are NOT retried; no call count assertion |
| 3 | `test_put_with_file_path` | **Medium** | Doesn't verify the file-reading code path was actually exercised vs. the bytes fallback |
| 4 | `test_retry_on_503/504/500` | **Low** | Missing call count assertions (inconsistent with `test_retry_on_502`) |
| 5 | (All retry tests) | **Medium** | No test verifies exponential backoff sleep durations; `mock_sleep` fixture doesn't expose the mock |
